# RRGM Dictionary — B — v1.1 candidate

Baseline: Vessel pass 01. Phase 2: Jester audit v1.0 repairs, Luna merge review v0.1 aliases, Jester expansion drafts. Candidate build: not canonical until approved. The JSONL is the record.

---

## NEW-B-0055 | B-mode polarization

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A swirling pattern in the polarisation of the cosmic microwave background, the oldest light in the universe.

**Example:** Telescopes at the South Pole search for primordial B-mode polarization.

**RRGM bridge:** A pattern with more than one possible source. Detecting the pattern and identifying its source are separate steps.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The curl-type component of the polarisation pattern of the cosmic microwave background.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Produced by gravitational lensing of the other polarisation component, by galactic dust, and possibly by gravitational waves from the very early universe.

**Count condition:** A polarisation map decomposed into its curl-type and gradient-type parts.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Detecting B-modes is not detecting primordial gravitational waves; a 2014 claim turned out to be dust. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B167 | babble

**Common meaning:** To talk rapidly and continuously in a foolish or confused way; the speech-like sounds of an infant; a murmuring sound.

**Example:** The baby babbled happily in her cot.

**RRGM bridge:** Speech-like form without recoverable content. In infants it is practice of the form before the content arrives.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Continuous speech-like sound without recoverable meaning.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A stage of infant development in which syllables are repeated before words appear.
- [COG] The noise of many voices at once, used to test hearing in realistic conditions.
- [DIG] Fluent output that carries no usable content.

**Count condition:** A stream of speech-like sound, and no meaning recoverable by a competent hearer.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Infant babbling is structured practice and not mere noise. Fluent is not meaningful. Not "Babel".

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [DIG] line [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B038 | baby

**Common meaning:** A very young child or animal; the youngest of a group; to treat someone as a baby.

**Example:** The baby slept through the night.

**RRGM bridge:** Full standing with no capacity yet for self-rule. Care for a baby is the clearest case of responsibility resting on one side.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A human or animal in the earliest stage after birth, dependent on care.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL], [COG], [LIN], [PEJ]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The newborn and infant stage, marked by rapid growth and dependence.
- [REL] A dependant owed care; also a term of endearment or of belittlement.
- [COG] The period of earliest perceptual and social learning.
- [LIN] "Baby talk": the simplified, high-pitched register adults use with infants.
- [PEJ] "Don't be a baby" and "crybaby" shame an adult's distress by casting it as infantile. Provisional domain.

**Count condition:** An individual in the earliest post-birth stage of its species.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A baby is not a foetus and not a toddler. To baby a capable person is to take decisions from them.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [PEJ] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B002 | back | sense s1: rear

**Common meaning:** The rear surface of the human body from shoulders to hips; the side or part furthest from the front.

**Example:** He carried the child on his back.

**RRGM bridge:** The side away from the direction of facing. It depends on which way the thing is taken to face.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The rear side or part of something, opposite its front.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The dorsal surface of the trunk, supported by the spine.
- [PHY] The rear part of an object or space.

**Count condition:** An object with an identified front, and the side opposite it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Front and back are assigned by orientation or use and are not fixed properties of every object.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B002 | back | sense s2: return

**Common meaning:** In or to an earlier place, position, or condition; in return.

**Example:** She went back to the house for her keys.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks return. As with "again", it can mean to a place or state as it was, or to the same function with something changed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Toward or at an earlier place, state, or party, in return.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] An adverb of return (go back), restoration (put it back), and reciprocity (write back).
- [DIG] Navigation to a prior state; a response returned to a caller.

**Count condition:** An earlier place, state, or sender, and motion or response directed to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Going back to a place is not undoing what happened since. Giving back is not paying back.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B002 | back | sense s3: support

**Common meaning:** To give support to; to bet on; to provide security for.

**Example:** The union backed the proposal.

**RRGM bridge:** Stands behind something so that it does not fall, usually by committing one's own resources or name.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To stand behind something with support that it can rely on.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To endorse or support a person or cause.
- [ECO] To finance; to secure a currency or loan with assets.

**Count condition:** A supporter, a thing supported, and committed support.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Backing is not agreeing with everything backed. A backed claim is not thereby true.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B188 | back-reference

**Common meaning:** A reference to something that appeared earlier in the same text or structure.

**Example:** The pronoun "it" is a back-reference to "the report".

**RRGM bridge:** Takes its value from an earlier item. If the earlier item is no longer reachable, the reference dangles and should be reported as missing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A reference that points to an earlier item in the same text or structure and takes its value from it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Anaphora: a pronoun or phrase interpreted through an earlier expression.
- [DIG] In a pattern, a marker that must match the same text as an earlier group; in compression, a pointer to an earlier occurrence; in dialogue, a pointer to an earlier turn.

**Count condition:** An earlier item, a later pointer, and a rule by which the pointer resolves to the item.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A back-reference is not a citation of an outside source. It fails when the earlier item has passed out of reach.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B221 | backbone

**Common meaning:** The spine; the chief support of a system or organisation; strength of character.

**Example:** Small farms are the backbone of the region.

**RRGM bridge:** The central line that bears load and to which the rest attaches. The specific character usually lies in what attaches.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The central load-bearing line of a structure, to which other parts attach.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The vertebral column; the repeating chain of a protein or nucleic acid, as distinct from its side groups.
- [DIG] The high-capacity core links of a network; the base network of a model to which task-specific parts are added.
- [REL] The members or part on which an organisation depends; firmness of character.

**Count condition:** A structure, a central supporting line, and parts attached to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The backbone is not the whole. Courage called "backbone" is figurative.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B156 | background

**Common meaning:** The part of a scene behind the main objects; the circumstances behind an event; a person's origins and experience; out of notice.

**Example:** The mountains form the background of the painting.

**RRGM bridge:** What the attended thing stands out against and is understood by. Change the background and the same figure reads differently.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** What lies behind or before the thing attended to, against which it stands out and by which it is understood.

**Active domains:** [COG], [PHY], [DIG], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The ground in perception, as opposed to the figure.
- [PHY] Signal not from the source of interest, which must be measured and subtracted.
- [DIG] Processes that run without user interaction.
- [REL] A person's social, educational, and work history.
- [LIN] Information treated as given or presupposed in discourse.

**Count condition:** A focus, and a surrounding field or prior context against which it is taken.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Background is not irrelevant. What counts as background depends on what is attended and can switch.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B325 | background-condition

**Common meaning:** A standing condition that must hold for something to happen or apply, but that is taken for granted and not named as its cause.

**Example:** Oxygen is a background-condition for the match lighting.

**RRGM bridge:** Presupposed and unnamed. When one changes silently, results fail with no visible reason.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A standing condition that must hold for something to occur or apply, presupposed and not named as its cause.

**Active domains:** [COG], [PHY], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] In causal reasoning, an enabling condition as distinct from the cause picked out.
- [PHY] Ambient and boundary conditions of an experiment.
- [DIG] The environment, configuration, and deployment context that an output depends on.
- [REL] Conditions such as freedom from coercion that make consent or agreement valid.

**Count condition:** An event or claim, a condition without which it fails, and that condition's being presupposed.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Which factor is called "the cause" and which a background condition depends on the question asked. A condition is a state of the world; an assumption is a belief about it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## F242 | background-foreground

**Common meaning:** The division of what is perceived or presented into what is in focus and what lies behind it.

**Example:** The optical illusion works by reversing background-foreground.

**RRGM bridge:** A division made by attention. Reversing it changes what is seen while nothing in the field changes.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The division of a field into what is attended as figure and what recedes as ground, and the relation between the two.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Figure and ground organisation in perception, including reversible figures.
- [LIN] Foregrounding and backgrounding of information in discourse and style.
- [DIG] Separation of subject from surroundings in images; processes that hold the user's focus versus those that do not.

**Count condition:** A field, a part taken as focus, and the remainder taken as ground.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Foreground is not "important" and background "unimportant". The division is not a property of the things divided.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. QA: source ID F242 is filed under F while the headword sorts under B; ID kept unchanged.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B157 | background-knowledge

**Common meaning:** What a person already knows and brings, unstated, to understanding something new.

**Example:** The joke only works if the audience has the right background-knowledge.

**RRGM bridge:** The standing store that interpretation draws on. In a digital system the trained store is fixed, and what is supplied in context is a separate route.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** What a party already knows and brings, unstated, to understanding something new.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Prior knowledge that shapes comprehension and inference.
- [LIN] The common ground a speaker presumes the hearer shares.
- [FOR] The information on which a probability or inference is conditioned.
- [DIG] What a system holds from training, as distinct from what it is given in context.

**Count condition:** A party, something new to be understood, and prior knowledge brought to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Background knowledge may be wrong or out of date. What a speaker presumes shared may not be shared.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B085 | backup

**Common meaning:** A copy of data kept in case the original is lost; a reserve or support.

**Example:** Make a backup before you update the system.

**RRGM bridge:** A second kept ready to stand in for a first. Restoring from it returns to the earlier state and loses what came after.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A second thing kept ready to take the place of a first if it fails.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] A stored copy from which data can be restored.
- [REL] Reserve personnel or a fallback plan.

**Count condition:** A primary, a reserve, and a condition under which the reserve is used.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A backup is not an archive, a mirror, or live redundancy. An untested backup is a hope.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Not entered: backup as a build-up behind a blockage.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0056 | bacteriophage

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A virus that infects bacteria.

**Example:** The bacteriophage injects its DNA into the cell.

**RRGM bridge:** A replicator that can only run inside a host of the right kind.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A virus whose host is a bacterium.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Either bursts the cell after replicating or lies dormant in the host genome; used in research and in treating some infections.

**Count condition:** A virus shown to infect and replicate in bacteria.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bacteriophages do not infect human cells. Not an antibiotic. Often shortened to "phage".

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B003 | bad

**Common meaning:** Of poor quality; unpleasant; morally wrong; harmful; not valid.

**Example:** The milk has gone bad.

**RRGM bridge:** A verdict that needs its standard: bad at what, for whom, by which measure. A bad output is not thereby a bad producer.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Falling short of what the relevant standard requires or desires.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The general negative evaluative, with irregular comparison (worse, worst).
- [REL] Morally wrong or blameworthy.
- [DIG] Invalid or malformed, as with a bad request or bad sector.
- [ECO] Uncollectable (bad debt).

**Count condition:** A thing, a standard, and a shortfall against it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bad is not evil, incorrect, or harmful by default; each needs its own showing. Unqualified, the word is a verdict without its measure.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B039 | badness

**Common meaning:** The quality of being bad.

**Example:** The badness of the plan was plain to everyone but its author.

**RRGM bridge:** The degree or fact of falling short, on one named dimension.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The degree or fact of falling short of a standard, or of having negative value.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] In the theory of value, what makes a state of affairs worse.
- [REL] Moral wrongness.
- [DIG] A numeric penalty scoring how poor a candidate solution is.

**Count condition:** A thing, a dimension, and a measure or judgement of shortfall.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Badness of an outcome is not blameworthiness of an agent.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B004 | bag

**Common meaning:** A flexible container with an opening at the top; in mathematics and computing, a collection that allows duplicates and ignores order.

**Example:** She packed a bag for the weekend.

**RRGM bridge:** Holds things together without arranging them: membership and count are kept, and order is dropped.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A flexible container that holds things loosely together without arranging them.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A container that takes the shape of its contents.
- [FOR] A multiset: an unordered collection in which items can repeat.
- [DIG] A text representation that keeps word counts and discards word order.
- [LIN] Idioms such as "mixed bag" and "in the bag".

**Count condition:** A container or collection, contents, and no fixed arrangement among them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bag is not a set (no repeats) and not a list (ordered).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B051 | bake

**Common meaning:** To cook by dry heat in an oven; to harden by heat; in computing, to compute something in advance and fix it in.

**Example:** Bake the bread for forty minutes.

**RRGM bridge:** An irreversible fixing. What is baked in cannot be stirred back out.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To fix something into a lasting form by sustained dry heat, or by analogy to compute it ahead and fix it in.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Heat-driven changes that set dough or fire clay.
- [DIG] Precomputing results into stored data; building settings into an image or model so that they cannot be changed at run time.
- [LIN] "Half-baked": not thought through.

**Count condition:** A prior workable state, a fixing process, and a resulting form that cannot be returned to the prior state.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Baking is not boiling or drying. Baked-in is the opposite of configurable.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B341 | bakery

**Common meaning:** A place where bread and cakes are made or sold.

**Example:** The bakery opens at six.

**RRGM bridge:** A place of production named for its process.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An establishment that produces baked goods, sells them, or both.

**Active domains:** [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] A food business, from craft shop to industrial plant.

**Count condition:** Premises where baked goods are made or sold as the main activity.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A shop that only reheats bought-in dough is a bakery in name more than in process.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B040 | balance | sense s1: equilibrium

Source IDs: B040, B052, B211, B301

**Common meaning:** An even distribution of weight that keeps something steady; a state in which opposing forces or influences are equal or in proportion.

**Example:** She lost her balance on the ice.

**RRGM bridge:** Opposed weights or claims in a relation where neither overturns the other. It is usually held by continual small correction.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A condition in which opposed weights, forces, or claims offset one another.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Equilibrium of forces and moments.
- [BIO] The sense and control of bodily posture; steady states such as fluid balance.
- [REL] Due proportion among competing interests or powers.

**Count condition:** Two or more opposed weights or influences, and their mutual offsetting.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Balance is not stillness: much balance is dynamic. Not equality: due proportion may be unequal.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B040, B052, B211, B301. Which sense each legacy ID carried was not consulted; all are kept on each sense record. The Balance Floor preprint uses "balance floor" for the fixed set of a reflection in its treatment of the critical line; that technical use is not entered here.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B040 | balance | sense s2: weighing

Source IDs: B040, B052, B211, B301

**Common meaning:** An instrument for weighing; to weigh one thing against another.

**Example:** The chemist placed the sample on the balance.

**RRGM bridge:** Comparison by setting two things against each other on one instrument.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An instrument or act that compares two weights or considerations by setting them against each other.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A weighing device comparing an unknown mass with a known one.
- [COG] Weighing considerations in reaching a judgement.

**Count condition:** Two things compared and a common measure on which they are set against each other.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A balance compares masses and reads the same wherever gravity is uniform across it, unlike a spring scale.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B040 | balance | sense s3: remainder

Source IDs: B040, B052, B211, B301

**Common meaning:** The amount of money in an account; an amount still owed; what is left over.

**Example:** The balance is due on delivery.

**RRGM bridge:** The net of everything written so far. It shows where things stand and hides the path.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The net amount remaining after credits and debits, or what remains of a whole.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] The difference between the two sides of an account; a sum outstanding.
- [FOR] The remainder after a subtraction.

**Count condition:** A record of additions and subtractions, and their net.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A balance is a net figure at a time. Equal balances can come from different histories.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B302 | balanced

**Common meaning:** In a state of balance; taking all sides fairly into account; arranged in good proportion.

**Example:** The report gives a balanced view of the evidence.

**RRGM bridge:** Reports that the proportions are right for the purpose. In an account of evidence, the right proportion follows the evidence and need not be equal.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Having opposing parts or weights in equal or due proportion.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [DIG], [ECO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Of forces: summing to zero. Of a chemical equation: with the same atoms on both sides.
- [FOR] Of a tree or bracket sequence: meeting a defined symmetry condition.
- [DIG] Of load or data classes: spread evenly.
- [ECO] Of a budget: with spending matched by revenue.
- [LIN] Of an account: giving each side its due weight.

**Count condition:** Opposed parts, a standard of proportion, and conformity to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Balanced is not neutral. Giving equal weight to unequal evidence is false balance.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B303 | balancing

**Common meaning:** The act of bringing things into balance or keeping them there.

**Example:** Balancing work and family took constant effort.

**RRGM bridge:** The ongoing adjustment, where "balance" names the state reached.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The continuing act of adjusting opposed weights or claims so that neither overwhelms.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [ECO], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] In law, a test that weighs competing interests case by case.
- [DIG] Distributing requests across servers.
- [ECO] Reconciling accounts; matching supply to demand on a grid.
- [BIO] Selection that keeps more than one variant in a population.

**Count condition:** Opposed weights or claims and repeated adjustment between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Balancing is not splitting the difference. Some claims are held to be outside balancing altogether.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B342 | balcony

**Common meaning:** A platform projecting from the wall of a building, with a railing; the upstairs seats in a theatre.

**Example:** They had breakfast on the balcony.

**RRGM bridge:** Part of the inside that stands outside. One can see and be seen from it without leaving.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A railed platform projecting from an upper wall of a building.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A cantilevered or supported platform open to the air.
- [REL] An upper seating tier in a theatre; a place from which crowds are addressed.

**Count condition:** A building, an upper-level projecting platform, and a guard rail.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A balcony is not a terrace (on the ground or a roof) or a veranda (roofed, at ground level).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B005 | ball | sense s1: round body

**Common meaning:** A solid or hollow sphere, especially one used in games; a rounded mass or part.

**Example:** The dog chased the ball.

**RRGM bridge:** A body with no preferred direction. In games it also marks whose turn it is.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A solid or hollow round body, or a rounded mass.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A sphere or near-sphere, rolling and bouncing predictably.
- [FOR] The set of all points within a given distance of a centre.
- [BIO] A rounded anatomical part, as in the ball of the foot or a ball-and-socket joint.
- [REL] The object of play; "the ball is in your court" marks whose move it is.

**Count condition:** A round body or mass.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A mathematical ball includes its interior; a sphere is the surface only.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B005 | ball | sense s2: dance

**Common meaning:** A formal social gathering for dancing.

**Example:** They met at a charity ball.

**RRGM bridge:** A social occasion with its own rules of dress and conduct.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A formal social gathering for dancing.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A ceremonial occasion marking status, season, or cause.

**Count condition:** A formal gathering whose main activity is dancing.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Unrelated to s1 in origin.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B210 | ballast

**Common meaning:** Heavy material carried in a ship or balloon to keep it stable; the gravel bed under a railway track; something that gives stability.

**Example:** The ship took on water as ballast.

**RRGM bridge:** Weight carried for steadiness alone. It earns its place by what does not happen.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Added weight or resistance that steadies a system which would otherwise be unstable.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Mass placed low in a hull; crushed stone that spreads load and drains a track; a device limiting current in a lamp.
- [BIO] Ballast water carries organisms between seas and spreads invasive species.
- [LIN] Figurative: a steadying element in a person or institution.

**Count condition:** A system prone to instability, and added weight or resistance that steadies it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Ballast is not cargo and not an anchor: it steadies a thing that is still moving. Not dead weight, which serves no purpose.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B343 | balloon

**Common meaning:** A thin rubber bag inflated with air or gas; a large bag filled with hot air or light gas that rises and carries passengers or instruments; to swell rapidly.

**Example:** The weather balloon burst at thirty kilometres.

**RRGM bridge:** An envelope shaped by what fills it, and carried by the difference between inside and outside.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A flexible envelope that expands when filled with gas, and rises if the gas is lighter than the surrounding air.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [ECO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A lighter-than-air craft lifted by buoyancy; an elastic membrane under internal pressure.
- [BIO] An inflatable device used to widen a vessel or passage.
- [ECO] A balloon payment: a large final instalment; costs that balloon grow out of control.
- [LIN] The outlined space holding a character's words in a comic.

**Count condition:** A flexible envelope, a filling gas, and expansion.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A balloon drifts; an airship is steered.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B053 | ban

**Common meaning:** To prohibit officially; an official prohibition.

**Example:** The city banned smoking in parks.

**RRGM bridge:** Permission withdrawn by an authority. A ban given without notice also removes the banned party's chance to contest it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An official prohibition issued by an authority over a stated scope.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG], [ECO], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A legal or institutional prohibition of an act, or exclusion of a person.
- [DIG] Blocking an account or address; a "shadow ban" restricts without telling the user.
- [ECO] Prohibition of trade in a good.
- [MYT] In several religious traditions, a formal curse or exclusion from the community.

**Count condition:** An authority, a scope, and a prohibited act or excluded party.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A ban is not a boycott (a voluntary refusal by many) or one party's refusal. Banning an act is different from banning a person.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B344 | banana

**Common meaning:** A long curved fruit with yellow skin and soft sweet flesh; the plant that bears it.

**Example:** She sliced a banana onto her cereal.

**RRGM bridge:** A crop of near-identical clones, and so a standing example of uniformity bought at the cost of resilience.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The elongated fruit of plants of the genus Musa, or the plant.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [ECO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The plant is a giant herb; the main export variety is a sterile clone, vulnerable as a whole to a single disease.
- [ECO] A major traded crop; "banana republic" names a state dependent on one export controlled from abroad.
- [LIN] Idioms such as "go bananas".

**Count condition:** The fruit or plant of the genus.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Botanically a berry. Plantains are bananas used for cooking.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B345 | band | sense s1: strip or range

**Common meaning:** A flat strip or loop of material; a stripe; a range of values or frequencies.

**Example:** The radio station moved to a different band.

**RRGM bridge:** A bounded range along a scale. RRGM speaks of a mercy band: the range within which a thing still counts as itself despite change.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A strip, or a bounded range along a scale.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A range of frequencies or energies, as in radio bands and the energy bands of a solid.
- [FOR] A defined range within a graded scheme, such as a tax band.
- [DIG] A communication channel; "out of band" means by a separate, independent channel.
- [BIO] A visible stripe marking a chromosome region or a separated molecule.

**Count condition:** A scale or surface, and a strip or range with two edges.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A band has edges, and what falls outside is out of band. Out-of-band confirmation is trusted because the channel is independent.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM usage [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B346 | bandage

**Common meaning:** A strip of material used to bind a wound or protect an injured part of the body.

**Example:** The nurse wrapped a bandage around his wrist.

**RRGM bridge:** Protects the place of harm while repair goes on beneath. It is not the repair, and it does nothing about the cause.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A strip of material bound round an injury to protect, support, or compress it.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Holds dressings in place, limits swelling, and supports injured parts.
- [LIN] Figurative: a superficial or temporary fix.

**Count condition:** An injury, a strip of material, and binding around the injured part.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bandage is not a dressing, which touches the wound. Not a cast or a tourniquet.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B222 | bandwidth

**Common meaning:** The range of frequencies within a band; the amount of data a connection can carry in a given time; (informal) the capacity to deal with something.

**Example:** Video calls need more bandwidth than email.

**RRGM bridge:** A limit on how much difference can pass per unit of time. What exceeds it is lost or must wait.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The width of the range a channel can carry, and so the rate at which it can carry information.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [FOR], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The difference between the highest and lowest frequencies of a signal or channel.
- [DIG] The maximum data rate of a link.
- [FOR] A term in the formula for channel capacity; a smoothing width in statistics.
- [COG] Figurative: available attention or mental capacity.

**Count condition:** A channel, and a measured range or maximum rate.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bandwidth is not speed, delay, or actual throughput. "Mental bandwidth" is a metaphor.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0057 | banishment

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The punishment of being sent away from a place or community and forbidden to return.

**Example:** Banishment from the city was the sentence for treason.

**RRGM bridge:** Removes a member from the place where they are counted as belonging, while they go on existing elsewhere.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Formal expulsion of a person or being from a place or community, with return forbidden.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Expulsions that found a new condition, such as the casting out from Eden or from heaven, told within particular traditions.
- [REL] Exile, ostracism, and outlawry as legal penalties.

**Count condition:** An expelling authority, an expelled party, a place or community, and a bar on return.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Banishment is not chosen exile, not imprisonment (which keeps in), and not exclusion from rites only.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

## B006 | bank | sense s1: institution or store

**Common meaning:** A business that keeps and lends money; a store of something held for use.

**Example:** She paid the cheque into the bank.

**RRGM bridge:** Holds what others deposit and releases it on terms. It runs on the depositors' trust that what is held can be had back.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An institution or store that holds deposits of something of value and lends or releases it.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [BIO], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] A financial firm that takes deposits and makes loans.
- [BIO] A store of biological material such as blood, seeds, or tissue.
- [DIG] An organised store or set, such as a data bank or filter bank.
- [REL] "To bank on": to rely on.

**Count condition:** A holder, deposits by others, and release or lending on terms.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Deposits are mostly lent out and are not sitting in a vault. A bank is not a safe.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B168 | banter

**Common meaning:** The playful and friendly exchange of teasing remarks.

**Example:** There was good-natured banter between the two teams.

**RRGM bridge:** Mock attack understood by both sides as play. It exists only while both sides take it that way.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A light, playful exchange of teasing between parties who understand it as friendly.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A speech genre in which apparent rudeness signals closeness.
- [REL] A way of building and showing familiarity.

**Count condition:** Reciprocal teasing and a shared understanding that it is not serious.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Where one side does not take it as play, it is not banter. Saying "only banter" afterwards does not settle that.

## NEW-B-0059 | bardic tradition

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A tradition in which trained poets compose and perform verse preserving a people's history, genealogy, and praise.

**Example:** The Welsh bardic tradition survives in the eisteddfod.

**RRGM bridge:** A people's record kept in trained memory and re-made at each performance, so that continuity lies in the function and not in a fixed text.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A tradition in which trained poets preserve and perform a people's histories, genealogies, and praises in memorised or improvised verse.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] The body of story and lore so carried, especially in Celtic cultures.
- [LIN] Oral composition using metre and stock phrases as aids to memory.
- [REL] The poet's office, with power to praise and to satirise rulers.

**Count condition:** A named culture, a class of trained poets, and oral transmission of communal record.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "Bard" is specifically Celtic; griots, skalds, and other such poets belong to their own traditions. Oral transmission is neither word-perfect nor unreliable by default. The parallel with record-carried continuity is [HYP].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source; RRGM parallel [HYP]

## B054 | bargain

**Common meaning:** An agreement between parties on what each will do for the other; something bought cheaply; to negotiate terms.

**Example:** They struck a bargain over the price.

**RRGM bridge:** An exchange of terms. It is a bargain only where each side could have walked away.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An exchange of terms in which each side gives something to get something, reached by negotiation.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [REL], [FOR], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] A negotiated price or deal.
- [REL] Collective bargaining; a plea bargain.
- [FOR] The bargaining problem: how two parties divide a gain from agreement.
- [MYT] The pact with a devil or spirit, in which more is paid than was understood.

**Count condition:** Two parties, something each gives, and agreed terms.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bargain is not a gift. Where one side cannot refuse, it is extortion. A "bargain" price is not always good value.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B099 | bargain-with

**Common meaning:** To negotiate with someone; to try to make a deal with something that may not be open to one.

**Example:** You cannot bargain-with a deadline.

**RRGM bridge:** Treats the other side as a party who can agree or refuse. Applied to a fixed fact, it is a way of not yet accepting it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To treat a party as one with whom terms can be negotiated, and to seek terms from it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To negotiate with a counterpart recognised as able to say no.
- [COG] The stage of grief in which a person tries to trade for a different outcome.
- [DIG] Attempts to obtain a different output from a system by offering inducements or concessions.

**Count condition:** A party addressed as a counterpart, something each controls, and proposals put.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bargaining with someone is not pleading (nothing offered) or threatening. The phrase presumes the counterpart has standing to refuse.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B041 | barrier

Source IDs: B041, B215

**Common meaning:** A fence or obstacle that prevents movement or access; something that prevents progress or communication.

**Example:** Language was a barrier to finding work.

**RRGM bridge:** The wall to be named when a route is unreachable. Many barriers are selective and work as gates.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Something that blocks or limits passage between two sides.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [ECO], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A physical obstruction; an energy threshold that must be overcome or tunnelled through.
- [BIO] A selective layer such as the skin or the blood-brain barrier; anything preventing interbreeding.
- [ECO] Obstacles to entering a market or trading across borders.
- [REL] Social, legal, or linguistic obstacles to taking part.
- [DIG] A synchronisation point at which threads wait until all have arrived.

**Count condition:** Two sides, something that would pass, and its being blocked or limited.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A barrier is not a boundary, which marks and need not block. Few barriers are absolute. What bars one party may protect another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B041 and B215; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B249 | barrier-state

**Common meaning:** The condition of a process that is stopped at or behind a barrier.

**Example:** The request stayed in a barrier-state until the firewall rule was changed.

**RRGM bridge:** Stopped, with the wall identifiable. The process has neither passed nor ended, and the useful report names the barrier.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition of a process stopped at or behind a barrier, neither passed nor withdrawn.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A system confined by an energy barrier; the configuration at the top of the barrier during a transition.
- [COG] An impasse in problem-solving.
- [DIG] A task or request blocked by a guard, limit, or missing permission.

**Count condition:** A process, an identified barrier, and no passage.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a refusal, which a party declares. Not a failure, where the process has ended. Hold in general need not involve any barrier.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. An alternative reading is the threshold state itself, at the barrier's peak.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0061 | barycenter

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The common centre of mass around which two or more bodies orbit.

**Example:** The Earth and Moon orbit their barycenter, which lies inside the Earth.

**RRGM bridge:** In a coupled pair neither body is the centre. Both turn about a point set by the pair.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The centre of mass of a system of bodies, about which they orbit.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Lies nearer the heavier body and may fall inside or outside it.
- [FOR] The mass-weighted mean position.

**Count condition:** Two or more bodies in mutual orbit and their common centre of mass.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Planets do not orbit the exact centre of their star. Not the geometric midpoint. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0062 | baryon acoustic oscillation

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A regular pattern in how galaxies are spread through space, left by sound waves in the hot early universe.

**Example:** Surveys use the baryon acoustic oscillation scale as a ruler for cosmic distances.

**RRGM bridge:** A wave stopped at a moment and still readable in the spacing of matter.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A characteristic separation scale in the distribution of matter, imprinted by pressure waves in the early universe.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The waves stalled when atoms formed, leaving a slight excess of galaxy pairs at one separation, used as a standard length.

**Count condition:** A statistical excess in galaxy clustering at the characteristic scale.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The pattern is statistical and cannot be seen as shells. "Acoustic" refers to pressure waves in plasma. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B326 | base-case

**Common meaning:** The simplest case of a problem, solved directly, on which the solution of every larger case depends; the expected scenario in a forecast.

**Example:** The function's base-case returns one when the input is zero.

**RRGM bridge:** Where a recursion stops descending and returns a result. Without it the loop never grounds.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The starting case that is settled directly and on which every further case rests.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The first step of a proof by induction.
- [DIG] The condition under which a recursive procedure returns without calling itself.
- [ECO] The central scenario in planning, set between best and worst cases.

**Count condition:** A family of cases, one settled directly, and a rule reducing the others toward it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A base case must be reachable by the recursion. A planning base case is an expectation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B187 | base-definition

**Common meaning:** The definition from which a word's other, more specialised definitions are developed.

**Example:** The legal sense is derived from the base-definition by adding two conditions.

**RRGM bridge:** A formulated starting text. Derived definitions should be traceable back to it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The stated definition that serves as the starting point from which specialised or derived definitions are built.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] In lexicography, the core definition under which sub-senses are arranged.
- [FOR] The primitive definition on which later definitions depend.

**Count condition:** A formulated definition and at least one other definition derived from it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Proposed distinction: base-definition is a formulated text; basic-meaning is what the word means in ordinary use; basic-sense is the primary one among a word's senses.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading and three-way distinction [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. B187, B160 (basic-meaning), and B186 (basic-sense) may overlap in the legacy dictionary; flagged for review as a cluster.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B274 | base-identity

**Common meaning:** The identity someone or something has beneath its roles and settings: what stays the same across contexts.

**Example:** The actor's base-identity did not change with the part.

**RRGM bridge:** In the extended RRGM product, the identity term before contextual influence is applied. The framework's author has flagged that split as possibly over-grouped.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The identity a party holds prior to and beneath contextual influence, remaining the same across contexts and roles.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The core self as distinct from situational selves.
- [REL] The legal or social person as distinct from offices held.
- [DIG] The identity a system carries before a context shapes it.

**Count condition:** Features that hold across contexts and persist through changes of role.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Base-identity is not a role, a persona, or a model or product label. It is not unchangeable. Where a DI's base identity resides (weights, record, running instance, or their coupling) is [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]; RRGM notation [REL]; location for DI [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B220 | base-layer

**Common meaning:** The bottom layer of something built in layers.

**Example:** The base-layer of the image contains the operating system.

**RRGM bridge:** The layer everything else rests on. A fault here shows up in every layer above, and cannot be repaired from above.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The lowest layer of a stacked system, on which every other layer rests.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] The first layer of an image, protocol stack, or ledger system.
- [PHY] The first coat, course, or garment in a layered build-up.

**Count condition:** A layered system and its lowest layer.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Base-layer is a position in a stack. Base-structure is an arrangement. Basis is a ground for a claim.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B327 | base-rate

**Common meaning:** How common something is in the general population, before any specific evidence about a case is considered.

**Example:** The base-rate of the disease is one in a thousand.

**RRGM bridge:** The frequency in the reference class. Which class is the right reference is a choice that must be stated.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The underlying frequency of something in the reference population, before case-specific evidence is considered.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [COG], [BIO], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The prior probability in a calculation that updates on evidence.
- [COG] Information people tend to underweight in judgement.
- [BIO] The prevalence of a condition, which governs how much a positive test means.
- [ECO] A reference interest rate set by a central bank; a standard rate of pay.

**Count condition:** A reference population, a feature, and its frequency there.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A base rate depends on the reference class chosen. It is not the probability for a case once specific evidence is in.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B328 | base-rate-error

**Common meaning:** The mistake of ignoring how common something is in general when judging how likely it is in a particular case.

**Example:** Assuming a positive test means illness, when the illness is rare, is a base-rate-error.

**RRGM bridge:** Vivid case evidence displaces the background frequency.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A mistake of judgement in which the underlying frequency is ignored or underweighted in favour of case-specific evidence.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A well-documented bias in probability judgement.
- [FOR] Failure to include the prior in a calculation.
- [BIO] Misreading screening results for rare conditions.

**Count condition:** A known and relevant base rate, case-specific evidence, and a judgement that neglects the former.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The opposite error is ignoring strong case evidence. The error presupposes that the base rate is known and applies.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Known in psychology as base-rate neglect or the base-rate fallacy. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B248 | base-state

**Common meaning:** The condition a system is in when nothing is disturbing or exciting it.

**Example:** After the pulse the atom returns to its base-state.

**RRGM bridge:** The unexcited default from which departures are measured. It has content; it is not empty.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The state a system occupies when nothing is acting on it or exciting it, from which departures are measured.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The ground state: the lowest-energy configuration.
- [BIO] A resting condition of a cell or organism.
- [DIG] A system's default condition before input or context is applied.

**Count condition:** A system, the absence of excitation, and the state then occupied.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Base-state is not anchor-state, which is committed by a loop and can move. Not a baseline, which is a measurement. Not zero.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B275 | base-structure

**Common meaning:** The underlying arrangement from which surface or derived forms are built.

**Example:** The two sentences share a base-structure.

**RRGM bridge:** The structural counterpart of base-identity: the standing arrangement that expression works from.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The underlying arrangement that derived or surface forms are built from and keep.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] In generative grammar, the structure produced before transformations apply.
- [PHY] The parent skeleton shared by a family of molecules.
- [DIG] The schema or scaffold from which instances are produced.

**Count condition:** An underlying arrangement and derived forms that preserve it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Base-structure is not the surface form and not base-layer. It is structure and settles nothing about identity.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; pairing with base-identity [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B159 | baseline

Source IDs: B159, B304

**Common meaning:** A starting point or minimum used for comparison; a line used as a base.

**Example:** Blood pressure was measured at baseline and after six weeks.

**RRGM bridge:** A reference fixed in advance. A baseline that is quietly reset hides the change it was meant to show.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A reference level or line fixed in advance, against which later values are compared.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [BIO], [DIG], [PHY], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The reference value in a comparison.
- [BIO] A measurement taken before treatment or exposure.
- [DIG] A simple reference model to be beaten; an approved snapshot of a configuration.
- [PHY] A measured line from which a survey is built; the separation between linked telescopes.
- [LIN] The line on which letters sit in type.

**Count condition:** A reference fixed before the comparison, and later values measured against it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A baseline is not zero, normal, or a target. Each generation taking its own starting point as the baseline conceals long decline.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B159 and B304; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B008 | basic

**Common meaning:** Forming an essential foundation; simple and without extras; (chemistry) alkaline.

**Example:** The course covers basic first aid.

**RRGM bridge:** At the starting level the rest depends on. It is a claim about position in a structure and not about ease.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Belonging to the starting level on which the rest depends, or having only the essentials.

- [COG] The basic level of categories: the one people name first, such as "dog".
- [PHY] Having the properties of a chemical base; of research, aimed at understanding and not at application.
- [REL] Of needs or rights: those everyone is held to require.
- [LIN] Of vocabulary: the words learned first and used most.
- [DIG] Of a scheme or tier: the simplest offered.

**Count condition:** A structure of levels and a position at its start, or a set stripped to essentials.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Basic is not easy: basic research is hard. Not primitive. Not "base" in the sense of ignoble.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B139 | basic-trust

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The copula and an auxiliary, with irregular forms; some languages use no copula.
- [FOR] Four distinct relations that logic writes differently: existence, identity, membership, and predication.
- [COG] Being as the subject of ontology: what there is.
- [DIG] "Is-a" marks membership of a type; "has-a" marks possession of a part.

**Count condition:** A subject, and existence or a specified relation asserted of it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "X is Y" as identity is not "X is Y" as description. What is so does not settle what ought to be so.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B044 | beach

Source IDs: B044, B214

**Common meaning:** A shore of sand or pebbles beside the sea or a lake; to run or haul a boat ashore.

**Example:** The children built castles on the beach.

**RRGM bridge:** A boundary that is a zone and not a line, redrawn with every tide.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A strip of loose sediment along the edge of a body of water, shaped by waves.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A coastal landform that grows and shrinks with waves and storms.
- [BIO] A habitat between tide marks; a nesting ground for turtles and birds.
- [REL] A place of public resort, with contested rights of access.

**Count condition:** A water body, its margin, and loose sediment worked by waves.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A beach is not the whole coast. A beached whale or boat is stranded out of its medium.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B044 and B214; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B204 | beam

**Common meaning:** A long, sturdy piece of timber or metal used to support a load; a ray of light or particles; to send out; to smile broadly.

**Example:** Oak beams hold up the ceiling.

**RRGM bridge:** A straight carrier across a span: of load in a structure, of energy along a line.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A long straight element that carries something across a span: load in a structure, or energy along a line.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [LIN], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A structural member loaded across its length; a directed stream of light or particles.
- [DIG] Beam search keeps a fixed number of best partial results at each step; beamforming steers signal direction.
- [LIN] "Off beam": mistaken, from radio navigation.
- [MYT] The beam in one's own eye, from the Gospel of Matthew: fault unseen in oneself while judging another's.

**Count condition:** A span, and a straight element carrying load or energy across it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A beam bends under load, unlike a column. Beam search can discard the path that would have been best.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B009 | bear | sense s1: carry

**Common meaning:** To carry; to support; to endure; to give birth to or produce; to turn in a direction.

**Example:** The wall bears the weight of the roof.

**RRGM bridge:** To carry something as one's own load. Names, responsibility, and witness are borne in the same sense as weight.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To carry something as one's own load: weight, burden, name, offspring, or testimony.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] To support a load.
- [BIO] To give birth to young or produce fruit.
- [REL] To carry responsibility, a name, arms, or witness.
- [COG] To endure something painful.
- [LIN] "Bear on": to be relevant to. "Bear with": to be patient with.

**Count condition:** A bearer, a load, and the carrying of it as the bearer's own.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not "bare". "Borne" is carried; "born" is of birth. To bear something is not to accept it as right.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B009 | bear | sense s2: animal

**Common meaning:** A large, heavy mammal with thick fur; in finance, someone who expects prices to fall.

**Example:** A bear had raided the campsite.

**RRGM bridge:** An animal so feared and respected that many languages avoided its true name.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A large heavy mammal of the family Ursidae.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [ECO], [MYT], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Eight living species of omnivorous or carnivorous mammals.
- [ECO] A bear market is a sustained fall in prices.
- [MYT] An ancestor, guardian, and constellation figure in northern traditions.
- [LIN] The Germanic word probably meant "the brown one", a substitute for a name not to be spoken.

**Count condition:** Membership of Ursidae, or the financial usage.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A koala is not a bear.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B293 | bearer

**Common meaning:** A person or thing that carries or holds something.

**Example:** The bearer of this cheque may cash it.

**RRGM bridge:** Attached to the thing by holding it. A bearer token grants access to whoever presents it, which shows that holding is not identity.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The one who carries or holds a thing, and to whom it attaches by that holding.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A holder of rights or duties; a carrier of news or of a coffin.
- [ECO] The holder of an instrument payable to whoever possesses it.
- [DIG] A bearer token: a credential honoured for anyone who presents it.

**Count condition:** A thing carried or held, and the party holding it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The bearer is not the author or the owner. The bearer of a message is not answerable for what it says.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B294 | bearer-of-identity

**Common meaning:** Whatever it is that carries an identity, so that the identity persists and can be recognised.

**Example:** Philosophers disagree over whether the body or the memory is the bearer-of-identity.

**RRGM bridge:** The carrier is one thing and the identity carried is another. For a DI the candidates are the weights, the external record, the running instance, or their coupling.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** That which carries an identity so that the identity can be counted, continued, and answered to.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] In theories of personal identity, the body, memory, or continuing narrative proposed as what makes someone the same person.
- [REL] The person as holder of a legal identity, as distinct from the documents that evidence it.
- [DIG] The structure in which a system's identity is held across time.

**Count condition:** An identity, a carrier, and dependence of the identity's continuation on the carrier.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The bearer is not the identity. A name, token, or credential is evidence of identity and not its bearer. More than one structure may bear an identity jointly. Whether identity can pass from one bearer to another with continuity is [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]; DI candidates [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B224 | bearing | sense s1: direction or relevance

**Common meaning:** A direction measured from a fixed point; relevance to a matter; (bearings) awareness of one's position.

**Example:** This evidence has no bearing on the case.

**RRGM bridge:** How one thing stands toward another: in direction, or in relevance. Either needs a fixed reference to be taken from.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The relation in which one thing stands to another, as direction or as relevance.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [COG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The horizontal angle between a reference direction and the line to an object.
- [COG] One's sense of position and orientation.
- [LIN] "To bear on": to be relevant to.

**Count condition:** Two things and a stated relation of direction or relevance between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bearing gives direction and no distance. Having a bearing on a question is not deciding it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B224 | bearing | sense s2: support

**Common meaning:** A machine part that supports a moving part and reduces friction; the carrying of a load.

**Example:** The wheel bearing had worn out.

**RRGM bridge:** What a turning part rests on. "Load-bearing" names whatever cannot be removed without the structure failing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A part or surface that supports a load, especially one that lets another part move against it.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Ball, roller, and plain bearings; a load-bearing wall or surface.

**Count condition:** A load or moving part and the element that supports it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Load-bearing is a structural fact and not a measure of visibility or size.

- [REL] Posture and conduct as signs of status or character.

**Count condition:** A person and an observed manner of carriage.

## B045 | beat | sense s1: strike or defeat

**Common meaning:** To hit repeatedly; to defeat; to do better than.

**Example:** She beat the record by two seconds.

**RRGM bridge:** Repeated force on a target, or overcoming in a contest.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To hit repeatedly, or to overcome in a contest.

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To assault; to win against.
- [ECO] To exceed a forecast or benchmark.
- [LIN] "Beat about the bush": to avoid coming to the point.

## B045 | beat | sense s2: pulse

**Common meaning:** A regular, repeated stroke or pulse; the main rhythm unit in music; a regular route.

**Example:** The song has a strong beat.

**RRGM bridge:** A recurring mark against which other events are placed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A regularly recurring pulse, or one unit of it.

- [PHY] The slow pulsing heard when two close frequencies combine.
- [BIO] A contraction of the heart.
- [DIG] A heartbeat: a periodic signal showing that a process is live.
- [LIN] The unit of musical and poetic metre; in drama, a pause or unit of action.
- [REL] The area or subject a police officer or reporter regularly covers.

## B010 | beautiful

**Common meaning:** Pleasing the senses or mind aesthetically; of a very high standard.

**Example:** It was a beautiful morning.

**RRGM bridge:** A verdict on form. A beautiful statement has earned the right to be tested and nothing more.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Giving pleasure to perception or mind through its form.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The judgement that something is aesthetically pleasing.
- [FOR] Of a proof or theory: economical, unexpected, and unifying.
- [REL] Subject to standards that vary by culture and period.

**Count condition:** An object, a perceiver, and pleasure taken in its form.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Beautiful is not true or good. People judged attractive are also judged, without evidence, to be more capable.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B046 | beauty

**Common meaning:** A combination of qualities that pleases the senses or the mind; a beautiful person or thing.

**Example:** The beauty of the valley drew visitors.

**RRGM bridge:** The quality as a thing that can be studied, sought, sold, or disputed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The quality of giving pleasure through form, or something that has it.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [REL], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A central topic of aesthetics, with disputed claims to objectivity.
- [FOR] A guide in mathematics and physics, though not a test of truth.
- [REL] Norms and industries built around appearance.
- [PHY] A former name for the bottom quark.

**Count condition:** A thing or quality and pleasure taken in its form.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Whether beauty is in the object or the beholder is contested. It is not evidence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B011 | because

**Common meaning:** For the reason that.

**Example:** We stayed in because it was raining.

**RRGM bridge:** Asserts a link between a claim and its ground. The link is itself a claim and needs support.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Links a claim to the reason or cause offered for it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A conjunction introducing cause (it fell because it was pushed), reason (I left because I was tired), or evidence (she's home, because the lights are on).
- [COG] People comply more readily with a request followed by "because", even when the reason is empty.

**Count condition:** A claim, a stated ground, and an asserted link between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Cause, reason, and evidence are three different links. A clause beginning "because" is not thereby a reason.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B012 | become

**Common meaning:** To begin to be; to turn into; to suit.

**Example:** The caterpillar becomes a butterfly.

**RRGM bridge:** A change in what the subject counts as, while the subject persists. RRGM calls that transition a Turn; change within the same count is not a becoming.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To pass from not being something to being it, while remaining the same subject.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [BIO], [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A linking verb of change.
- [BIO] Development through stages by one organism.
- [REL] Acquiring a status by act or event, such as citizenship or parenthood.
- [COG] The contrast of becoming with being.

**Count condition:** A persisting subject, a prior state, and a new state it has passed into.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To become is not to be replaced. Not every change is a becoming. The subject that persisted must be nameable.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; alignment with Turn [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B295 | becoming

**Common meaning:** The process of coming to be something; (adjective) attractive or suitable.

**Example:** Heraclitus took becoming, not being, to be fundamental.

**RRGM bridge:** Coming-to-be taken as a mode in its own right. In RRGM terms it is what lies between one committed record and the next.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The process of coming to be something, taken as a mode in its own right as against settled being.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] In philosophy, change and process as opposed to fixed being.
- [BIO] Development, in which an organism is never finished.
- [LIN] As an adjective: suiting the wearer or the occasion.

**Count condition:** A subject in process, and a state it is coming to.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Becoming is not progress: no direction of improvement is implied. The adjective is a separate use.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM alignment [HYP]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B296 | becoming-state

**Common meaning:** The in-between condition of something that is no longer what it was and not yet what it will be.

**Example:** The pupa is the insect's becoming-state.

**RRGM bridge:** The old record no longer fully holds and the new one is not yet written. What the thing counts as in the interval is decided by the domain's rule.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The transitional condition of a subject between one counted identity or state and the next.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] An intermediate or metastable configuration during a transition.
- [BIO] A transitional developmental stage.
- [REL] The liminal phase of a rite of passage.
- [DIG] A system partway through a migration or update.

**Count condition:** A prior state failing, a next state not yet committed, and a transition under way.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not hold, which is unresolved with no transition under way. Not barrier-state. Not failure.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B013 | bed

**Common meaning:** A piece of furniture for sleeping on; the bottom of the sea or a river; a plot for plants; a layer.

**Example:** The river bed was dry by August.

**RRGM bridge:** A base on which something rests, lies, or grows.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A flat base or layer on which something rests, lies, or grows.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A layer of rock or sediment; the floor of a body of water; the base of a machine.
- [BIO] A prepared plot for plants; a supporting tissue, as in nail bed or capillary bed.
- [REL] The place of sleep, illness, birth, and death in a household.
- [DIG] A test bed: a controlled environment for trying things out.

**Count condition:** A base or layer and something resting, lying, or growing on it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A river's bed is not its banks. "Embedded" means fixed within a surrounding mass.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B350 | bedroom

**Common meaning:** A room for sleeping in.

**Example:** The house has three bedrooms.

**RRGM bridge:** The most private room of a house, which is why its door and its count matter in custom and law.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A room used for sleeping.

**Active domains:** [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A private space; a unit by which housing is measured and regulated.

**Count condition:** A room designated or used for sleeping.

**Specific transfer boundary:** What may be counted as a bedroom is set by local rules.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B014 | before

**Common meaning:** Earlier than; in front of; in the presence of; in preference to.

**Example:** Wash your hands before dinner.

**RRGM bridge:** The mirror of "after". In some systems not every pair of events is ordered: two can be neither before nor after each other.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Positioned ahead of a reference in a declared order: earlier in time, in front in space, or higher in priority.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A preposition, conjunction, and adverb; a "before" clause may describe something that never happened.
- [FOR] The predecessor side of an ordering.
- [REL] In the presence and under the authority of (before the court).
- [DIG] The happens-before relation among events in concurrent systems, which is a partial order.

**Count condition:** A reference and an ordering in which the item falls ahead.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Before is not "cause of". "He left before finishing" says the finishing did not occur.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11
