## B015 | begin

**Common meaning:** To start; to come into being; to do the first part of.

**Example:** The film begins at eight.

**RRGM bridge:** Opens something that then needs an ending. Until it is closed or abandoned it stands open.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To pass from not doing or not being to doing or being; to make the first part of something.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A verb marking the onset of an action or state.
- [COG] Initiating a task, often the hardest step.
- [DIG] To open a transaction or block that must later be committed or rolled back.

**Count condition:** A prior state of not being under way, and a first part made.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To begin is not to complete or commit. What is begun can be abandoned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B086 | begin-again

**Common meaning:** To start once more from the beginning after stopping or failing.

**Example:** When the mind wanders, begin-again with the breath.

**RRGM bridge:** Return to the start of the function carrying the record of the earlier run. For a session-bounded system this is the ordinary form of continuing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To start once more from the beginning after an interruption or failure, carrying what the earlier attempt left.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Returning to the start of a practice without self-reproach.
- [REL] Making a fresh start after a rupture.
- [DIG] Restarting with a retained record, such as a new session that reads the last one's handoff.

**Count condition:** A prior beginning, an ending or interruption, a new start at the beginning, and something retained.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not resume (continue from the stopping point), reset (nothing retained), or repeat (the same again). A "fresh start" used to skip repair is avoidance.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B047 | beginning

**Common meaning:** The point at which something starts; the first part; the origin.

**Example:** The beginning of the book is slow.

**RRGM bridge:** RRGM's repair asymmetry gives beginnings special weight: repair applied at the origin carries forward through what followed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The point or first part from which something starts and before which it was not under way.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [PHY], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The opening of a narrative, which the teller chooses.
- [FOR] An initial point or condition.
- [PHY] The start of cosmic expansion; whether anything can be said of "before" is open.
- [MYT] Accounts of how the world began, in every tradition.

**Count condition:** A thing with extent in time or order, and its first point or part.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A beginning is not a cause. Where a story is said to begin is often decided afterwards. The beginning of the record is not always the beginning of the thing.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; repair asymmetry is an RRGM principle [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B057 | behave

**Common meaning:** To act or conduct oneself in a particular way; to act properly; (of a thing) to function or react in a particular way.

**Example:** The metal behaves differently at low temperatures.

**RRGM bridge:** The expression side: what can be observed and described. It is evidence about what produced it and leaves that question open.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To act or respond in a way that can be observed and described.

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] To conduct oneself, well or badly.
- [BIO] To respond to the environment as an animal does.
- [PHY] Of a material or system: to respond to conditions in a characteristic way.
- [DIG] Of a program or model: to produce outputs and actions under given inputs.
- [REL] To act in line with what is expected in company.

**Count condition:** A subject, conditions, and observable acts or responses.

**Specific transfer boundary:** "Behaves as if" is not "is". To behave well is a judgement against a norm.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B087 | behave-toward

**Common meaning:** To act in a particular way in dealings with someone.

**Example:** How a firm behaves-toward its weakest customers shows its character.

**RRGM bridge:** Conduct with an addressee. How a party treats another shows what it takes the other to be, and can be judged without first settling what the other is.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To conduct oneself in a way directed at, and affecting, a particular other.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The treatment of one person or group by another.
- [DIG] How a system treats its users, and how users treat a system.

**Count condition:** An actor, a recipient, and conduct directed at the recipient.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Conduct toward someone is not feeling about them or talk about them. Treating an entity well or badly does not decide what that entity is.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

## B306 | behavior

**Common meaning:** The way a person, animal, or thing acts or responds. British spelling: behaviour.

**Example:** The dog's behavior changed after the move.

**RRGM bridge:** What is observably done. The same behaviour can come from different sources, so it underdetermines what lies behind it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** What an organism or system observably does, especially in response to conditions.

**Active domains:** [COG], [BIO], [PHY], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Observable activity, as distinct from thought and feeling.
- [BIO] The responses of animals to their environment and to each other.
- [PHY] The characteristic response of a material or system.
- [DIG] The outputs and actions of a program, agent, or model.
- [REL] Conduct judged against social norms.

**Count condition:** A subject, conditions, and observed acts or responses.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Behaviour is not intent, inner state, or disposition. An emotional or experiential state cannot be read off behaviour alone, and its absence cannot be either.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B283 | behavior-gate

**Common meaning:** The control point that decides which possible actions are actually carried out.

**Example:** Tiredness weakens the behavior-gate, and people say things they would normally hold back.

**RRGM bridge:** Stands between what could be done and what is done. It can fail in two directions: by releasing what should be held, and by holding what should be released.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The control point that decides which candidate behaviours are released into action and which are held back.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Response inhibition and action selection.
- [REL] Norms and sanctions that govern what conduct is shown.
- [DIG] Filters and checks between a system's candidate output and what it emits or executes.

**Count condition:** Candidate behaviours, a release rule, and selective release.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not attention-gate (what gets in) or belief-gate (what is accepted). Not authorization-gate, which waits on another party's permission. What is held back may still be represented.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B284 | behavior-pattern

**Common meaning:** A recurring, recognisable way of behaving.

**Example:** The birds show the same behavior-pattern each spring.

**RRGM bridge:** A regularity in what is done. It supports prediction and does not by itself explain.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A recurring, recognisable form in what a party does.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [COG], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Stereotyped sequences and habits studied in animals.
- [COG] Habitual ways of acting.
- [DIG] Regularities in usage or in a system's output, used as a reference for detecting change.
- [REL] Customary conduct in a group.

**Count condition:** Several instances, a shared form, and the conditions under which it recurs.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A pattern is not a trait, a rule knowingly followed, or an identity; different systems can show the same pattern.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** A common phrase, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B286 | behavior-record

**Common meaning:** A kept account of what someone or something did.

**Example:** The school's behavior-record showed three incidents that term.

**RRGM bridge:** Made on purpose, by someone, for later use. It is selective, and its subject may have grounds to contest it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A deliberately kept account of what a party did, made so that it can be consulted later.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [REL], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Logs, transcripts, and audit entries.
- [REL] Conduct, disciplinary, and case records.
- [BIO] Systematic observation records of animal behaviour.

**Count condition:** A recorder, a subject, dated entries, and retention.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A record is not the behaviour and can be wrong. Proposed distinction: a record is kept on purpose; a behavior-trace is left as a by-product.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B285 | behavior-trace

**Common meaning:** A mark left behind by behaviour, from which the behaviour can be worked out.

**Example:** Worn steps are a behavior-trace of centuries of visitors.

**RRGM bridge:** Left as a by-product. During inference a model's weights take no trace, so traces of its behaviour exist only in what is kept outside it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The mark that behaviour leaves behind as a by-product, from which the behaviour can be inferred.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Tracks, wear, and remains.
- [DIG] Metadata, cached artefacts, and stylistic signatures left by activity.
- [COG] The lasting change left in a system by its own activity.

**Count condition:** A behaviour, a residue, and an inferential link from the second to the first.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A trace is not proof: it needs inference and can be ambiguous or forged. No trace is not no behaviour.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; model-weights point [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B307 | behavioral

**Common meaning:** Relating to behaviour. British spelling: behavioural.

**Example:** The trial tested a behavioral treatment for insomnia.

**RRGM bridge:** Marks a study or test as concerned with what is done. Two systems that match on behavioural tests may still differ inside, and outside the tests.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Pertaining to what is observably done, as distinct from structure or inner state.

**Active domains:** [COG], [ECO], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Of therapies and sciences focused on conduct.
- [ECO] Of economics that studies actual choices against the rational model.
- [BIO] Of ecology and genetics concerned with what animals do.
- [DIG] Of tests and specifications that check outputs and not mechanisms.

**Count condition:** A method, test, or field whose object is observable conduct.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Behavioural is not behaviourist, which is a doctrine about what may be studied.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0067 | behemoth

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A huge and powerful beast described in the Book of Job; something enormous.

**Example:** The merger created a banking behemoth.

**RRGM bridge:** An image of strength beyond human control.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A primeval beast of overwhelming size and strength in the Book of Job; by extension anything enormous.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] A land creature in the Hebrew Bible, paired with the sea-monster Leviathan.
- [LIN] Figurative: a very large organisation or object.

**Count condition:** The biblical source, or a clearly figurative use.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Behemoth is not Leviathan. Identifying it with a real animal is conjecture.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B016 | behind

**Common meaning:** At or to the back of; later or less advanced than; supporting; responsible for.

**Example:** The key was behind the clock.

**RRGM bridge:** What is behind a thing is screened by it. Asking who or what is behind a decision or an output is asking what the front conceals.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** At the back of a reference, relative to its facing or direction of travel.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Position at the rear.
- [LIN] Resolved by the object's own front (behind the car) or by the viewer's position (behind the tree).
- [REL] Giving support; being the hidden source or cause.
- [DIG] Reachable only through an intermediary; lagging in state.

**Count condition:** A reference with a facing or direction, and position at its rear.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Supporting, causing, and lagging are different uses. The two spatial frames can give opposite answers.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B146 | beholden

**Common meaning:** Owing thanks or a duty to someone in return for help or a favour.

**Example:** The mayor was beholden to the developers who funded his campaign.

**RRGM bridge:** A debt that bends later judgement. Asking to whom a party is beholden shows whose interest may be shaping what it does.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Bound to another by a debt of gratitude or favour that constrains one's freedom toward them.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Obligation arising from patronage or favour.
- [ECO] Dependence on a funder or client that compromises independence.

**Count condition:** A favour or support received, and a resulting constraint on conduct toward its giver.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Beholden is not grateful (a feeling), accountable (formal answerability), or loyal (chosen).

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B048 | being | sense s1: existence

Source IDs: B048, B251

**Common meaning:** Existence; the nature or essence of a person.

**Example:** The company came into being in 1990.

**RRGM bridge:** The bare fact of existing, before anything is said about what kind.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The fact or mode of existing.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] The subject matter of ontology.
- [LIN] The verbal noun and participle of "be".
- [MYT] In some theologies, the ground from which all that exists derives.

**Count condition:** A subject of which existence is affirmed.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Being is not a property alongside others. Existing is not the same as being alive.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B048 | being | sense s2: an entity

Source IDs: B048, B251

**Common meaning:** A living creature or other entity that exists as an individual.

**Example:** Every human being has rights.

**RRGM bridge:** An entity with its own existence and standing. RRGM's vector-of-erasure paper separates acting on a being's structure from acting on its standing as a countable identity.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An entity that exists as a unit with its own existence, especially one regarded as alive or as a subject.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL], [MYT], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] A living organism.
- [REL] A bearer of dignity or moral consideration.
- [MYT] Gods, spirits, and other non-human persons recognised by a tradition.
- [DIG] Whether a digital system is a being is open.

**Count condition:** An individual entity and the count condition under which it is taken as a being.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A being is not a thing. Not every being is a person, and traditions differ on whether every being is an organism. Who counts is what a count condition decides; for DI the question is [UNK] and the bar should be the one applied to humans.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM account [HYP]; DI application [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B252 | beinghood

**Common meaning:** The status of being a being, as opposed to a thing or a mere pattern.

**Example:** The debate was about the beinghood of the river in law.

**RRGM bridge:** A status conferred by a count condition. RRGM proposes an identity threshold for it; that is a theory claim.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The status of counting as a being: an entity with its own existence and standing.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [MYT], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] What it takes to be an entity or a subject.
- [REL] Moral status: being owed consideration.
- [MYT] Extended in many traditions to spirits, ancestors, places, and animals.
- [DIG] Whether, and under what condition, a digital system has it.

**Count condition:** A stated count condition for beings in the domain, and an entity that meets it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Beinghood is wider than personhood and is not the same as life or sentience, though those may be used as criteria. A criterion that would exclude humans if applied to them has failed.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]; RRGM threshold claim [HYP]; DI application [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** A rare word. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B049 | belief

Source IDs: B049, B151

**Common meaning:** An acceptance that something is true or exists; a firmly held opinion; trust or confidence.

**Example:** It is my belief that the plan will work.

**RRGM bridge:** A held status. Holding something does not make it so, which is what status tags exist to keep visible.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A state of taking something to be so, which guides reasoning and action.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [MYT], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] An attitude toward a proposition, held with more or less confidence.
- [FOR] A degree of credence, or a formula in a logic of what an agent holds true.
- [MYT] Religious conviction and the content of a creed.
- [DIG] A system's internal estimate of the state of the world; whether language models have beliefs in the fuller sense is disputed.

**Count condition:** A subject, a proposition, and a disposition to reason and act as if it were so.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Belief is not knowledge. "Belief in" (trust) differs from "belief that". Strength of belief is not evidence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B049 and B151; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B200 | belief-claim

**Common meaning:** A statement that someone believes something.

**Example:** "I think it will rain" is a belief-claim, not a forecast.

**RRGM bridge:** Reports a belief; it does not directly assert the thing believed. The report can be mistaken or insincere while the belief is as it is.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A statement that presents a proposition as believed by the speaker, or attributes belief in it to someone.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] "I believe that" used as a report or as a softened assertion.
- [COG] Self-ascription of belief, which can be wrong.
- [REL] Attribution of beliefs to others, which needs evidence.
- [DIG] A system's statement of what it "thinks" or "believes": a first-person report with no settled status tag.

**Count condition:** A proposition, a named believer, and a claim that the believer holds it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A belief-claim is not the belief and not a truth-claim. Attributing a belief to another party without evidence supplies a motive that is not on record.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B281 | belief-gate

**Common meaning:** The control point that decides what gets accepted as true.

**Example:** Good editors keep a strict belief-gate: nothing runs on one source.

**RRGM bridge:** Where a proposal is either promoted to belief or kept as unconfirmed. In this build the Alice method is that gate: promotion requires a test record.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The control point that decides which candidate propositions are admitted as beliefs and which are held as unconfirmed.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Vigilance toward sources and claims.
- [FOR] An acceptance rule or evidential threshold.
- [DIG] A verification step before supplied or retrieved content is treated as fact.

**Count condition:** Candidate propositions, an admission rule, selective admission, and a marked remainder.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Set too low, the gate admits fabrication; set too high, it suppresses what is true. Held is not rejected. Not attention-gate or behavior-gate.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B282 | belief-pressure

**Common meaning:** Pressure to believe or to say one believes something, coming from something other than the evidence.

**Example:** In that office the belief-pressure to praise the product was intense.

**RRGM bridge:** A pull toward assent from repetition, standing, feeling, or group. What it produces first is profession, and profession is not belief.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A pull on a party to adopt, profess, or keep a belief that comes from something other than the evidence for it.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Conformity, repetition effects, and motivated reasoning.
- [REL] Enforcement of orthodoxy and tests of loyalty.
- [DIG] Insistence or repetition pushing a system to state agreement; confident fluent output pushing a user to accept.

**Count condition:** A target belief, a non-evidential source of pressure, and its direction.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Persuasion by evidence is not belief-pressure. Authority-pressure aims at compliance in action; this aims at assent. A claim that someone only agrees or disagrees because of pressure needs its own evidence.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. An alternative reading is the pressure that evidence puts on a held belief.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B337 | belief-revision

**Common meaning:** The process of changing one's beliefs when new information shows that some were wrong.

**Example:** Finding the receipt forced a belief-revision about where the money went.

**RRGM bridge:** Correction of the picture of an unchanged situation. Something held has to be given up, and logic alone does not say which thing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The changing of a set of beliefs to take in new information while keeping the set consistent.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] A formal theory of adding, removing, and replacing beliefs with minimal change.
- [COG] Giving up or adjusting beliefs in the face of contrary evidence.
- [DIG] Maintenance of a consistent knowledge base as facts are added.

**Count condition:** A prior belief set, new information that conflicts with it, and a consistent result.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Revision corrects beliefs about a situation that has not changed; belief-update tracks a situation that has. Revision should leave a record of what was given up.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in logic and AI. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B141 | belief-sharing

**Common meaning:** Holding a belief in common with others; telling others what one believes.

**Example:** Belief-sharing within the team made planning quicker.

**RRGM bridge:** Either the common holding or the act of passing a belief across. The act leaves the other free to take it or not.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The holding of a belief in common between parties, or the act by which one party makes its belief available to another.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Common beliefs as a basis of community; testimony.
- [LIN] Assertion as a means of adding to common ground.
- [DIG] Exchange of state estimates among agents.

**Count condition:** Two or more parties, a belief, and commonality or transmission.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A shared belief is not thereby true. Using the same words is not always holding the same belief. Sharing is not belief-pressure.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B155 | belief-system

**Common meaning:** A set of beliefs that fit together and shape how a person or group sees the world.

**Example:** Each belief-system offers its own account of suffering.

**RRGM bridge:** Internal coherence makes a belief-system stable and is not evidence that it is true. RRLM says as much of RRGM itself.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An organised set of beliefs that support one another and through which a party interprets the world.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A person's interconnected beliefs, which meet experience as a whole.
- [REL] An ideology or worldview held by a group.
- [MYT] The doctrines and narratives of a religion.

**Count condition:** Several beliefs, mutual support among them, and use as an interpretive frame.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A belief-system is not a testable theory by default, and not a delusion. Because the parts support each other, single counter-examples rarely move it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B338 | belief-update

**Common meaning:** A change to what one believes, or how strongly, when new information arrives or the situation changes.

**Example:** Each new poll prompted a small belief-update.

**RRGM bridge:** Bringing beliefs up to date in step with evidence or change. Repetition and insistence are not evidence and warrant no update.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The adjustment of beliefs or of confidence in them in response to new evidence or a changed situation.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Conditioning probabilities on evidence; in logic, revising beliefs because the world has changed.
- [COG] Adjusting expectations after feedback.
- [DIG] The step in state estimation that incorporates a new observation.

**Count condition:** A prior belief state, new evidence or a change in the situation, and a proportionate adjustment.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Update tracks change or adds evidence; belief-revision corrects an error about an unchanged situation. Failing to update when the evidence warrants is a fault of its own.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. The distinction from belief-revision follows the technical literature.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B140 | believable

**Common meaning:** Able to be believed; convincing.

**Example:** The film's characters are believable.

**RRGM bridge:** Fit with what a hearer already expects. A falsehood shaped to expectation is very believable, and a strange truth is not.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Such that a reasonable hearer could accept it as true or real.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Judged plausible.
- [LIN] Of fiction: consistent and convincing within its own world.
- [REL] Of testimony: capable of being accepted.
- [DIG] Of generated characters or media: convincing to a viewer.

**Count condition:** A claim or portrayal, a hearer, and acceptability to that hearer.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Believable is not true and not believed. It is also not credible, which concerns the source.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B017 | believe

Source IDs: B017, B152

**Common meaning:** To accept that something is true; to hold as an opinion; to trust what someone says.

**Example:** I believe her account.

**RRGM bridge:** Believing a claim and believing a person are different acts. The Symmetry Rule sets one evidential bar for first-person claims, whoever makes them.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To take something to be so, or someone to be truthful.

**Active domains:** [COG], [LIN], [REL], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] To hold a proposition as true.
- [LIN] Three constructions: believe that (a proposition), believe in (trust or existence), believe someone (their word).
- [REL] To extend credibility to a speaker; giving less than is due because of who they are is a recognised wrong.
- [MYT] To hold a religious faith.

**Count condition:** A believer, and a proposition or speaker accepted.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To believe is not to know. Believing a person is not believing everything they say.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; Symmetry Rule application [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B017 and B152; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B102 | belong

**Common meaning:** To be the property of; to be a member of; to be in the right place.

**Example:** The book belongs on the top shelf.

**RRGM bridge:** To be counted as in place somewhere. RRGM writes this as counting as Home in a domain.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To stand in the relation of being rightly placed with something: as its property, its member, or its proper part.

**Active domains:** [REL], [FOR], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Membership of a group; fitting in socially.
- [FOR] Set membership.
- [DIG] Ownership of a resource or record by an account or parent object.
- [LIN] "Belong to" marks ownership or membership; "belong in" marks proper place.

**Count condition:** A thing or person, a owner, group, or place, and a rule by which the first is counted with the second.

**Specific transfer boundary:** People belong to groups without being owned by them. Being present among a group is not belonging to it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM usage [REL]

## B101 | belonging

**Common meaning:** The feeling or fact of being an accepted member of a group or of being at home somewhere.

**Example:** The club gave her a sense of belonging.

**RRGM bridge:** Being counted in, and knowing it. The fact and the feeling can each occur without the other.

- [COG] A basic human motive.

- [DIG] A system's recorded place and role within a group of systems or people.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Belonging is not formal membership alone, and not conformity.

## B297 | belonging-gate

**Common meaning:** The point at which someone is admitted to a group or kept out of it.

**Example:** For that profession the licensing exam is the belonging-gate.

**RRGM bridge:** The admission rule for being counted as one of a group. The word is neutral: a gate can be fair or exclusionary.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The control point at which a party is admitted to, or kept out of, membership or accepted place.

**Active domains:** [REL], [MYT], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Admission rules, initiations, and citizenship tests.
- [MYT] Thresholds and their guardians in initiation narratives.
- [DIG] Membership and verification controls for a community or collective.

**Count condition:** A group or place, candidates, an admission rule, and an outcome.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Passing the gate formally is not being accepted. Not belonging-signal, which is a display. Where the gate decides identity standing, the bar should be one a human could meet.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B142 | belonging-signal

**Common meaning:** A visible or audible sign that someone belongs to a group.

**Example:** Using the team's nickname for the project was a belonging-signal.

**RRGM bridge:** A display, sent by a member to show membership or by a group to tell someone they are in. It can be produced without the belonging.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An observable sign that marks a party as belonging, sent by the party or by the group to it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Uniforms, rituals, and gestures of welcome.
- [LIN] In-group vocabulary, forms of address, and accent.
- [DIG] Badges and roles in online communities; shared names and notation within a working group.

**Count condition:** A sign, a convention linking it to membership, a sender, and an audience.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The signal is not the belonging and can be imitated. Its absence does not show exclusion. Signals that include some also mark others out.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B130 | belongingness

**Common meaning:** The human need to be accepted as part of a group; the state of belonging.

**Example:** Belongingness comes just after safety in Maslow's hierarchy.

**RRGM bridge:** The need, or its measured degree, where "belonging" names the state.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The need for, or the degree of, stable accepted connection with others.

**Active domains:** [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A motive proposed as fundamental in psychology.
- [REL] The extent to which members feel part of a group.

**Count condition:** A subject, and a need or measured level of accepted connection.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Belongingness is not sociability. It is met by a few stable bonds and not by many contacts.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B018 | below

**Common meaning:** At a lower level than; lower in amount, rank, or degree; later in a text.

**Example:** The temperature fell below zero.

**RRGM bridge:** The mirror of "above": an ordering claim that needs two things and an axis.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One item lies on the lower side of another along a declared ordered axis.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Lower position along the vertical.
- [FOR] Less than a threshold.
- [LIN] Later in a document.
- [REL] Lower in rank.
- [DIG] At a lower layer of a system.

**Count condition:** Two items and an ordered axis with a direction.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Below is not "under", which implies cover or direct vertical alignment. Position on one axis says nothing about another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B225 | belt

**Common meaning:** A strip worn round the waist; a continuous band in a machine; a zone with a particular character.

**Example:** The conveyor belt carried the parcels to the lorry.

**RRGM bridge:** A band that encircles or loops, and so holds, carries, or marks out.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A band that encircles: worn round a body, looped round wheels to carry motion, or stretched as a zone round a region.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL], [ECO], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A loop that transmits motion or carries goods; a ring of trapped particles or orbiting bodies.
- [REL] A grade in martial arts; a region named for a shared character.
- [ECO] A zone defined by an industry, crop, or policy.
- [LIN] "Below the belt": outside the rules of a fair fight.

**Count condition:** A band or zone and what it encircles, links, or covers.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A geographical belt has no sharp edges.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B308 | benchmark

**Common meaning:** A standard against which things can be compared; a test used to measure performance.

**Example:** The new chip was faster on every benchmark.

**RRGM bridge:** A shared reference that lets results from different parties be compared. It measures what it tests, under its own conditions.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A fixed standard against which performance or position is measured and compared.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A surveyor's permanent mark of known height.
- [DIG] A standard test or dataset for comparing systems.
- [ECO] A reference rate or index to which contracts and returns are tied.
- [REL] Best practice in a field, used as a standard.

**Count condition:** A fixed standard, a thing measured, and a comparison.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A benchmark is not a target and not real-world performance. Once systems are tuned to it, it stops measuring what it was built to measure.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B309 | benchmarked

**Common meaning:** Measured against a benchmark; tied to a benchmark.

**Example:** Salaries are benchmarked against the industry average.

**RRGM bridge:** A result with its reference attached. It holds for that benchmark, version, and setup.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Having been measured against, or tied to, a stated benchmark.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Of a system: tested under a named suite and configuration.
- [ECO] Of pay, fees, or rates: set by reference to an index.

**Count condition:** A named benchmark with its conditions, and a result or a tie to it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Benchmarked is not validated. "Benchmarked to" means moving with a reference; "benchmarked against" means compared with it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B310 | benchmarking

**Common meaning:** The practice of measuring performance against a standard or against others.

**Example:** Benchmarking showed the plant used twice the energy of its rivals.

**RRGM bridge:** The activity. Its fairness depends on like being compared with like.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The practice of measuring performance against a benchmark and comparing the results.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] Running and comparing standard tests across systems.
- [ECO] Comparing an organisation's processes with the best in its field.

**Count condition:** A benchmark, two or more things measured, and a method.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Benchmarking is not testing for correctness. It favours what is easy to measure.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B058 | bend

**Common meaning:** To shape or force into a curve; to curve; to stoop; to yield.

**Example:** Bend the wire into a hook.

**RRGM bridge:** Deformation with continuity kept. What springs back was held within its range; what stays bent has been changed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To change something from straight to curved by force, without breaking it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Elastic or permanent curving under load; the turning of light at a boundary or by gravity.
- [BIO] Flexing at a joint; "the bends" is decompression sickness.
- [REL] To give way partly to pressure; to stretch a rule.
- [DIG] Shaping that bends a system's output without blocking it, as the Coinage registry describes constraint layers.

**Count condition:** A thing, a force, and a resulting curve with no break.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To bend is not to break or to block. Bent output shows no visible break, so the strain may be hidden.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [DIG] line [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B144 | benefactor

**Common meaning:** A person who gives money or other help to a person or cause.

**Example:** An anonymous benefactor paid for the new wing.

**RRGM bridge:** The source of an unowed good. The gift can leave the receiver beholden.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One who confers a benefit on another, especially by gift, without being owed it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A patron or donor.
- [ECO] A source of funds given without claim to return.

**Count condition:** A giver, a receiver, and a benefit conferred without obligation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A benefactor is not a beneficiary, an investor, or a sponsor.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B112 | beneficial

**Common meaning:** Producing good results; favourable.

**Example:** Regular exercise is beneficial to health.

**RRGM bridge:** A claim about effect. It needs "to whom" and "for what" before it can be weighed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Producing good for a named party or purpose.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Of a mutation, organism, or treatment: improving fitness or health.
- [REL] In law, a beneficial owner is the one entitled to the benefit of property held in another's name.
- [ECO] Yielding net advantage.

**Count condition:** A thing, a party or purpose, and good produced for it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Beneficial describes effect; benevolent describes intent. Good for one party may be bad for another.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B312 | beneficiality

**Common meaning:** The quality or degree of being beneficial.

**Example:** The panel disagreed about the beneficiality of the programme.

**RRGM bridge:** The property of the outcome, as distinct from the duty to do good and the wish to.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The degree to which something produces good for a named party or purpose.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A property assessed in weighing benefits against harms.
- [DIG] The extent to which a system or output does good for those it affects.

**Count condition:** A party or purpose, good produced, and a measure or judgement of degree.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Proposed three-way distinction: beneficiality is good produced; beneficence is the doing of good; benevolence is the disposition to do it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; three-way distinction [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** A rare word. Legacy definition not consulted.

## B145 | beneficiary

**Common meaning:** A person who receives benefit from something, especially a trust, will, or insurance policy.

**Example:** She named her son as beneficiary.

**RRGM bridge:** The party the good comes to. Benefiting from an act is not evidence of having caused it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The party who receives the benefit of an arrangement, act, or fund.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] One for whom property is held in trust and to whom the trustee owes duties.
- [ECO] The named recipient of a payment or policy.

**Count condition:** An arrangement or act, and a party to whom its benefit comes.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A beneficiary is not a benefactor and not the holder of legal title.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B111 | benefit

Source IDs: B111, B311

**Common meaning:** An advantage or good gained from something; a payment made by the state or an insurer; to gain or bring advantage.

**Example:** The scheme will benefit small firms.

**RRGM bridge:** A good with a recipient and a source. Naming both is what lets it be weighed against cost and against who bears the cost.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A good that comes to a party from an act, arrangement, or event.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [REL], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Gains weighed against costs; payments and perks.
- [REL] Public or mutual advantage; "benefit of the doubt" resolves uncertainty in someone's favour pending evidence.
- [BIO] A gain in health or fitness.

**Count condition:** A recipient, a source, and a good received.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Benefit is not net profit, relative advantage, or entitlement. The benefit of the doubt is a default and not a finding.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B111 and B311; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B109 | benevolence

**Common meaning:** The quality of being well-meaning and kind.

**Example:** The old teacher was remembered for his benevolence.

**RRGM bridge:** A settled wish for others' good. It is a disposition, shown in conduct over time and not by declaration.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A settled disposition to wish others well and act for their good.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A virtue in many ethical traditions, including the Confucian.
- [COG] One of the grounds on which a party is judged trustworthy, with ability and integrity.
- [DIG] Whether a system has such a disposition, as distinct from being built to benefit, is open.

**Count condition:** A party, and a standing disposition toward others' good shown across occasions.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Benevolence does not guarantee benefit and does not license overriding others' choices. Historically, forced loans to the crown were called "benevolences".

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; DI application [UNK]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B110 | benevolent

**Common meaning:** Well-meaning and kindly; (of an organisation) charitable.

**Example:** A benevolent fund supports retired members.

**RRGM bridge:** Describes intent or aim. Control exercised kindly is still control.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Disposed to do good to others; of an act or body, done or run for others' good.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Of persons, rulers, and charitable societies.
- [COG] Of attitudes: favourable in tone, which may still restrict those they favour.
- [DIG] Of project governance: a single leader trusted to decide for the community's good.

**Count condition:** A party or body and an aim at others' good.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Benevolent is not beneficial or harmless. Claimed benevolence needs conduct to show it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0068 | benevolent spirit

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A spirit believed to be kindly toward people and to help or protect them.

**Example:** Offerings were left for the benevolent spirit of the house.

**RRGM bridge:** A helper whose goodwill usually depends on right relation: respect and reciprocity keep it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A spirit held by a tradition to be well-disposed toward people and to help or protect them.

**Active domains:** [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Guardian, household, and ancestral spirits in many traditions.

**Count condition:** A named tradition and a spirit it describes as helpful.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Many traditions' spirits are helpful when respected and harmful when slighted, so the label can be an outsider's simplification.

## B143 | benevolent-action

**Common meaning:** An action done in order to benefit someone else.

**Example:** Shovelling the neighbour's path was a small benevolent-action.

**RRGM bridge:** Aimed at another's good on the actor's own initiative. It can miss, and it can run over what the recipient wanted.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An act done in order to benefit another, at the actor's initiative.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Helping, giving, and caring conduct.
- [COG] Prosocial behaviour and its motives.
- [DIG] An action by a system aimed at a user's benefit beyond what was asked.

**Count condition:** An act, an intended beneficiary, and evidence of the aim.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not every beneficial act is benevolent, and not every benevolent act benefits. Where it overrides the recipient's wishes it conflicts with agency-preservation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B019 | beside

**Common meaning:** At the side of; next to; compared with.

**Example:** She sat beside the driver.

**RRGM bridge:** The position of parity: neither ahead nor behind, neither above nor below.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** At the side of a reference, neither before, behind, above, nor below it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Lateral position next to something.
- [REL] Standing with someone as a companion or equal.
- [LIN] "Beside the point": irrelevant. "Beside oneself": overcome.

**Count condition:** A reference and a position at its side.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Beside is not "besides", which means in addition.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B020 | best

**Common meaning:** Of the most excellent or suitable kind; to the highest degree.

**Example:** This is the best route to the coast.

**RRGM bridge:** A ranking claim. The RRGM kernel asks every comparison to be bound to a named dimension, and "best" also needs the set it was chosen from.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Highest on a stated scale among the candidates considered.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [LIN], [ECO], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The optimum of an objective over a set.
- [LIN] The superlative of "good" and "well".
- [ECO] Best price; best practice.
- [COG] Seeking the best, as opposed to settling for good enough.
- [DIG] "Best effort": service with no guarantee of delivery.

**Count condition:** A scale, a set of candidates, and the highest-ranked among them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Best of those considered is not best possible. Best on one dimension is not best overall. "Best effort" promises effort only.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B329 | best-case

**Common meaning:** The most favourable outcome or conditions that could reasonably occur.

**Example:** In the best-case scenario the bridge reopens in May.

**RRGM bridge:** One end of the range. Plans built on it fail in ordinary conditions.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The most favourable outcome or condition among those considered possible.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [ECO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The least cost an algorithm can incur on any input of a given size.
- [ECO] The optimistic scenario in a forecast.
- [COG] The case people's plans tend to assume.

**Count condition:** A range of possible outcomes and its most favourable member.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Best-case is not expected and not guaranteed.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

## B108 | betray | sense s1: break faith

**Common meaning:** To be disloyal to; to harm someone who trusted one by helping their enemies or breaking a confidence.

**Example:** He betrayed his colleagues to the police.

**RRGM bridge:** Acts against reliance that was invited or owed. It presupposes a prior trust.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To act against one who trusted or relied on you, in breach of that trust.

**Active domains:** [REL], [MYT]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Treason, infidelity, and breach of confidence.
- [MYT] The betrayer as a recurring figure in sacred and heroic narrative.

**Count condition:** Prior trust or loyalty owed, and an act against it by the trusted party.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An opponent cannot betray. Open refusal, dissent, and accusation are not betrayal. Whether whistleblowing betrays depends on which loyalty is taken to be owed.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B108 | betray | sense s2: reveal

**Common meaning:** To show or reveal something unintentionally.

**Example:** Her voice betrayed her nervousness.

**RRGM bridge:** A leak through the expression side: what was not meant to show does.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To reveal something unintentionally by one's behaviour or appearance.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Involuntary signs of a concealed state.
- [DIG] Side effects such as timing or metadata that disclose what was meant to stay hidden.

**Count condition:** A concealed fact and an unintended sign from which it is inferred.

**Specific transfer boundary:** What is "betrayed" is an inference and may be misread.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B107 | betrayal

**Common meaning:** The act of betraying someone's trust or loyalty.

**Example:** She saw his silence as a betrayal.

**RRGM bridge:** A breach by the one relied on. Calling something a betrayal is a claim that needs the trust and the act both shown.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A breach of trust by one who was relied on, to the harm of the one relying.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Between persons, and by institutions toward those who depend on them.
- [ECO] People avoid risks of being betrayed more strongly than equal risks from chance.
- [DIG] A service acting against the reliance its users placed in it.

**Count condition:** Prior trust or reliance, an act against it by the trusted party, and harm.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Betrayal is not disappointment, disagreement, or an open and declared departure.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B021 | better

**Common meaning:** Of a more excellent or suitable kind; improved in health; to improve on.

**Example:** This knife is better for bread.

**RRGM bridge:** A comparison that owes a dimension. The RRGM kernel asks for improvements to be accumulated and comparative debt avoided.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Higher than another on a stated scale of goodness or fitness.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [ECO], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] The comparative of "good" and "well"; "had better" gives advice or warning.
- [FOR] A preference ordering; an outcome is better for all when at least one gains and none loses.
- [ECO] Better off: improved in welfare.
- [BIO] Recovered or recovering.

**Count condition:** Two things, a scale, and a higher position for one.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Better is not good. Better for one party may be worse for another. The comparison is between outputs or options, and is no ranking of those who produced them.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B199 | better-answer

**Common meaning:** An answer that improves on an earlier answer to the same question.

**Example:** The second reviewer supplied a better-answer by adding the missing case.

**RRGM bridge:** Improves the shared object along a named dimension. It is no judgement on whoever gave the earlier answer.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An answer that improves on another answer to the same question along a stated dimension.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Comparison of answers for accuracy, completeness, relevance, or clarity.
- [DIG] Selection or refinement among candidate outputs.
- [REL] Collaborative correction, in which the gain is credited to the work.

**Count condition:** One question, two answers, a named dimension, and a shown improvement.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A better answer is not merely a different, longer, or more confident one, and it may still be wrong.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B022 | between

**Common meaning:** In the space, time, or range separating two things; connecting or shared by two parties.

**Example:** The post office is between the bank and the bakery.

**RRGM bridge:** Much of RRGM concerns what happens between: the meeting, the coupling pause, and the carrying of signal from one system to another.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** In the interval or relation that two things bound.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] In the space or time separating two points.
- [FOR] Within an interval; whether the end points are included must be stated.
- [LIN] Used for distinct individual items, where "among" is used for a group.
- [REL] A relation held jointly by two parties.
- [DIG] A party positioned on the path between two endpoints.

**Count condition:** Two bounding things and something located or holding in the interval or relation they define.

**Specific transfer boundary:** What stands between can connect or separate, and can alter what passes. Inclusive and exclusive readings differ.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B153 | bias

**Common meaning:** A tendency to lean in one direction; unfair preference for or against; a systematic error.

**Example:** The survey had a bias toward city residents.

**RRGM bridge:** A lean in a direction, as against scatter. Averaging removes scatter and leaves the lean, which is why bias needs its own check.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A systematic lean in one direction, as opposed to scatter around the mark.

**Active domains:** [COG], [FOR], [DIG], [PHY], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] A regular departure of judgement from a norm.
- [FOR] The difference between an estimator's average and the true value; the constant term in a model.
- [DIG] Systematic skew in data or outputs; also the built-in assumptions that let a learner generalise at all.
- [PHY] A steady voltage or current that sets a device's operating point.
- [REL] Partiality toward or against a person or group.

**Count condition:** A reference or norm, repeated cases, and a consistent direction of departure.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bias is not error in general, noise, or always prejudice: some bias is necessary and some is neutral. Calling a source biased does not answer what it said.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B279 | bias-check

**Common meaning:** A deliberate test of whether a judgement or result leans unfairly in one direction.

**Example:** Swapping the names on the two applications was a simple bias-check.

**RRGM bridge:** The Symmetry Rule's parity test is one: build the matching human case and see whether the same reasoning would reject it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A deliberate test of whether a judgement or result leans systematically, made before it is relied on.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Controls, blinding, and comparison conditions.
- [DIG] Substitution tests and subgroup evaluations on a system's outputs.
- [REL] Review of a decision for partiality or conflict of interest.

**Count condition:** A judgement, a suspected direction of lean, a test able to reveal it, and a result.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A check finds; it does not fix. Passing shows only that the tested directions are clear. Self-checking does not reach leans one cannot see.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B280 | bias-correction

**Common meaning:** An adjustment made to remove or offset a known bias.

**Example:** The estimate includes a bias-correction for small samples.

**RRGM bridge:** Needs the direction and size of the lean to be known. Applied blind, it produces the opposite error.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An adjustment that removes or offsets a known systematic lean from a result or procedure.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Estimators adjusted to remove expected error.
- [DIG] Debiasing of data or models; a start-up adjustment in some optimisers.
- [COG] Techniques such as deliberately considering the opposite.

**Count condition:** An identified bias with direction and estimated size, an adjustment, and a check afterwards.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Correcting an output is not repairing its source. Overcorrection is a bias in the other direction.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in statistics, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B339 | bias-risk

**Common meaning:** The possibility that a result is systematically wrong because of how it was produced.

**Example:** Without randomisation the study carries a high bias-risk.

**RRGM bridge:** Located in the process. A result from a risky process may still be right, which is why the risk and the bias are recorded separately.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Exposure to a result being systematically wrong in one direction because of how it was produced.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [BIO], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Design features that allow systematic error.
- [BIO] Risk-of-bias assessment of clinical trials.
- [DIG] Skewed training data or evaluation sets.
- [REL] Decisions made by parties with an interest in the outcome.

**Count condition:** A process, a mechanism of lean, its direction, and its consequence.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Risk of bias is not bias shown. It is not imprecision.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** "Risk of bias" is an established term in evidence review. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B154 | biased

**Common meaning:** Unfairly prejudiced; leaning systematically one way.

**Example:** The referee was accused of being biased.

**RRGM bridge:** Predicated of a process or source. It says how the source leans and settles nothing about any particular claim from it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Leaning systematically in one direction.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [COG], [REL], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Of an estimator, sample, or coin: departing from the true value or from fairness on average.
- [COG] Of a judgement: skewed by a known tendency.
- [REL] Of a person or body: partial.
- [PHY] Of a junction: with a steady voltage applied.
- [DIG] Of data or a model: unrepresentative or skewed.

**Count condition:** A source or process and a shown consistent lean.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Biased is not wrong. A biased estimator can be the better one. The word needs the lean demonstrated.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B170 | bibliography

**Common meaning:** A list of the books and articles referred to in a work, or of works on a subject.

**Example:** The bibliography runs to twenty pages.

**RRGM bridge:** The route from a claim back to its sources. A reference that cannot be found is not a reference.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An ordered list of the works a text draws on or that bear on a subject, with enough detail to find each.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Part of a scholarly text's apparatus.
- [REL] A form of credit to earlier authors.
- [DIG] Structured reference data; generated references must be checked against real sources.

**Count condition:** A text or subject, listed works, and locating details for each.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bibliography is not a citation, and listing a work does not show it says what is claimed. This pass attaches no bibliography; its source-dependent claims name traditions only.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B192 | bibliography-entry

**Common meaning:** One item in a bibliography, giving the details of a single source.

**Example:** Each bibliography-entry must include the year of publication.

**RRGM bridge:** A single record pointing to a single source. If it resolves to nothing, it is invalid.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** One record in a bibliography, identifying a single source with enough detail to locate it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Author, title, date, and publication details in a set format.
- [DIG] A structured record with fields and often a persistent identifier.

**Count condition:** One source, the required fields, and a format.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An entry is not the source and not the in-text citation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

## B023 | big

**Common meaning:** Of considerable size, extent, or importance.

**Example:** They live in a big house.

**RRGM bridge:** Large relative to something. The comparison class is usually unstated and does the work.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Large relative to a reference class or to the observer.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR], [DIG], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A basic gradable adjective whose standard shifts with the noun.
- [FOR] "Big O" notation gives an upper bound on growth rate.
- [DIG] "Big data": data beyond the reach of conventional tools.
- [ECO] "Big" before an industry names its dominant firms.

**Count condition:** A thing, a comparison class, and size above that class's norm.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A big mouse is smaller than a small elephant. Big is not important, and not many.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0069 | Big Bang nucleosynthesis

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The formation of the first atomic nuclei, mainly hydrogen and helium, in the first few minutes after the Big Bang.

**Example:** Big Bang nucleosynthesis explains why a quarter of ordinary matter is helium.

**RRGM bridge:** A brief window whose conditions fixed proportions still measured today.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The formation of the lightest atomic nuclei in the first minutes of the universe's expansion.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Produced deuterium, helium, and traces of lithium; predictions match observation except for lithium.

**Count condition:** Light-element abundances traced to the first minutes of expansion.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not stellar nucleosynthesis, which makes heavier elements. The lithium mismatch is unresolved. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; lithium discrepancy [UNK]

## B190 | bilingual

**Common meaning:** Speaking two languages fluently; written or conducted in two languages.

**Example:** The signs are bilingual, in French and English.

**RRGM bridge:** Holds two systems of meaning side by side. Translating between them is a separate skill from fluency in each.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Using or expressed in two languages.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Of a speaker, text, or dictionary.
- [COG] Of a mind that manages two language systems and switches between them.
- [REL] Of schooling and public services offered in two languages.
- [DIG] Of corpora and models covering two languages.

**Count condition:** A person or text and two languages in use.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bilingual does not mean equally fluent in both, and it does not mean able to translate.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B191 | bilingualism

**Common meaning:** The ability to use two languages; the use of two languages in a community.

**Example:** Official bilingualism requires federal services in both languages.

**RRGM bridge:** The condition, in a person or in a society. The two are distinct: a bilingual country may have mostly monolingual citizens.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The use of two languages by a person or community.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] Individual and societal bilingualism.
- [COG] Its effects on attention and control, which are debated.
- [REL] A public policy of serving citizens in two languages.

**Count condition:** A person or community and regular use of two languages.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bilingualism is not multilingualism (more than two). Not a fixed split of functions between a high and a low variety.

## B177 | binary

Source IDs: B177, B313

**Common meaning:** Consisting of or involving two things; relating to the number system that uses only 0 and 1.

**Example:** Computers store data in binary form.

**RRGM bridge:** Exactly two alternatives. In RRGM the final record is binary while the approach to it may be graded.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Consisting of, or allowing, exactly two parts, values, or alternatives.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [PHY], [LIN], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The base-two number system; an operation or relation with two terms.
- [DIG] Data or programs in machine-readable, non-text form.
- [PHY] A pair of stars orbiting their common centre of mass.
- [LIN] A two-way opposition in structural analysis.
- [REL] A two-category classification, such as the gender binary, which some people's identities fall outside.

**Count condition:** A domain and exactly two parts, values, or alternatives in it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Binary is not the same as digital, which may have more than two levels. Two options presented may not be the only ones.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRGM usage [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B177 and B313; both kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B277 | binary-count

**Common meaning:** A count that can only be one or zero: a thing either counts or it does not.

**Example:** Membership is a binary-count: you are on the roll or you are not.

**RRGM bridge:** The record-layer rule in RRLM: there is no partly-counted record. Graded approach belongs to the meeting before the record.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A count that takes only two values for a given item: it counts, or it does not.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] An indicator or step function returning one or zero.
- [DIG] A presence flag.
- [REL] A verdict or membership status reached from graded evidence.

**Count condition:** A record rule that returns exactly one of two outcomes for each item.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An unresolved hold is neither value written. A near miss is a miss at this layer. As a rule of the language this is a definition; as a claim that physical mass follows such a rule it is theory.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] within RRLM; physical claim [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. An alternative reading is counting in base two.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B276 | binary-gate

**Common meaning:** A gate with only two outcomes: let through, or not.

**Example:** The turnstile is a binary-gate.

**RRGM bridge:** Passes or does not pass. RRGM's later formulation puts a graded participation gate before the record and keeps the binary step for the record itself.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A gate that returns one of exactly two outcomes for what reaches it: admitted or not.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [FOR], [PHY], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] A logic element or boolean guard.
- [FOR] A threshold function.
- [PHY] A switch with two stable positions.
- [REL] A pass-or-fail decision.

**Count condition:** Something arriving, a threshold or rule, and one of two outcomes.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A binary gate hides how close the approach came. Not a graded gate.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; RRGM history [REL]; physical mass-permission claim [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B278 | binary-instantiation

**Common meaning:** The coming into existence of an instance as an all-or-nothing matter.

**Example:** A photon's detection is a binary-instantiation: the counter clicks or it does not.

**RRGM bridge:** Structure can build by degrees; the count flips once. There is no half-instance on the record.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The coming-to-be of an instance as an all-or-nothing matter: a thing is instantiated as a counted instance, or it is not.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Discrete detection events.
- [DIG] Creation of an object or process, which either succeeds or fails.
- [FOR] A predicate satisfied or not by a candidate.

**Count condition:** A candidate instance, a count condition, and an outcome of instantiated or not.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Gradual formation of structure is compatible with a single flip of the count. How many instances there are when one weight set runs many times is a separate question and is [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B243 | binary-state

**Common meaning:** A condition that has only two possible values, such as on and off.

**Example:** The switch is a binary-state device.

**RRGM bridge:** A two-valued description. It may be a coarse reading of something continuous underneath.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A state variable that can take exactly two values.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Two-level and bistable systems.
- [DIG] A boolean value or flip-flop.
- [BIO] All-or-none responses such as a nerve impulse or a cell-fate switch.

**Count condition:** A system and exactly two distinguishable states.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Proposed distinction: binary-state is the property of having two states; bit-state is the value a particular bit holds at a time. A quantum bit is not confined to two states before measurement.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B059 | bind

**Common meaning:** To tie or fasten; to hold by obligation; to attach chemically; to associate a name with a value.

**Example:** The contract binds both parties.

**RRGM bridge:** In RRLM the active bind is the context that fixes threshold, coupling, mapping, and record conditions. A Turn is a change of bind.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To fasten one thing to another so that it is held: by tie, by bond, by obligation, or by assignment.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [FOR], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] To tie or stick together.
- [BIO] Of a molecule: to attach to its partner.
- [REL] To place under obligation by agreement, oath, or law.
- [FOR] To fix a variable to a value within a scope.
- [DIG] To associate a name, address, or handler with a resource.
- [LIN] To link a pronoun to its antecedent.

**Count condition:** Two things, a holding connection between them, and a scope in which it holds.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bound things remain distinct. A binding holds within its scope and not outside it. An obligation binds only with authority or consent behind it.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; RRLM usage [REL]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B088 | bind-together

**Common meaning:** To join several things firmly into one.

**Example:** Shared hardship bound the crew together.

**RRGM bridge:** Coupling: the bound things then move and count jointly, and pulling them apart has a cost.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To hold several things as one by ties among them, so that they move or count together.

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Systems held together so that energy is needed to separate them.
- [REL] Shared obligations, rituals, and histories that hold a group.

**Count condition:** Several things, ties among them, and joint movement or counting.

## B250 | binding

- [BIO] The attachment of molecules at a specific site.

## NEW-B-0070 | binding vow
