**Common meaning:** A solemn promise believed to bind the person who makes it with sacred or supernatural force.

- [REL] Religious and marriage vows recognised by a community.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A vow promises one's own conduct; an oath calls a witness to a statement. Some heroic taboos are imposed by others and not vowed.

## NEW-B-0072 | biodiversity

## NEW-B-0073 | biofilm

## B317 | biological-intelligence

**Common meaning:** Intelligence as found in living creatures.

**Example:** The octopus is a striking case of biological-intelligence that evolved separately from ours.

- [BIO] Nervous systems shaped by evolution, bound to one body and its energy budget.
- [COG] Human and animal cognition.
- [DIG] The contrast class for digital intelligence.

## S422 | biological-sex

**Common meaning:** The classification of living things as male or female according to their reproductive biology.

**Example:** In birds, biological-sex is determined by a different chromosome system from that of mammals.

**RRGM bridge:** A classification with one defining criterion in biology and several correlated markers. Disputes usually arise when the markers are used in place of the criterion, or when the classification is applied outside biology.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The classification of organisms by reproductive role, typically by the type of gamete produced.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Defined by gamete type; in humans, chromosomes, gonads, hormones, and anatomy usually align and sometimes do not. Some species are both sexes or change sex.
- [REL] A category recorded in law and medicine, distinguished in much current usage from gender.
- [LIN] The qualified phrase arose to mark the contrast with gender.

**Count condition:** An organism of a sexually reproducing species and its reproductive classification.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Sex and gender are distinguished in most current scholarly usage, though usage varies and is contested. Sex-related traits are several and do not always agree. The category does not apply to entities without reproductive biology.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. QA: source ID S422 is filed under S while the headword sorts under B; ID kept unchanged.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Flora, fauna, and microbes of a region, habitat, or geological period.

**Count condition:** A place or period and the organisms living in it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Biota is not biomass (their mass) or a biome (a community type with its environment).

## B024 | bit | sense s2: binary digit

Source IDs: B024, B178

**Common meaning:** The smallest unit of data in computing, with a value of 0 or 1; a unit of information.

**Example:** A byte is eight bits.

**RRGM bridge:** One settled yes-or-no. It is the smallest thing that can be written.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A binary digit, or the amount of information that settles one two-way choice.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The unit of information: the uncertainty removed by one equally likely binary choice.
- [DIG] A storage element with two states.
- [PHY] Erasing a bit has a minimum energy cost, so information is physical.

**Count condition:** A two-way distinction, stored or resolved.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A stored bit may carry less than one bit of information. Not a byte and not a qubit.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B244 | bit-state

**Common meaning:** The value a bit currently holds: 0 or 1.

**Example:** The error flipped the bit-state from 0 to 1.

**RRGM bridge:** A physical level read against a threshold and written as one of two values. The same bit-states mean different things under different encodings.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The value held by a bit at a given time: one of its two states.

**Active domains:** [DIG], [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [DIG] The stored value of a binary element.
- [PHY] A charge, voltage, or magnetisation level interpreted as 0 or 1.

**Count condition:** A bit, a time, and a read value.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bit-state is not the information it encodes. Between thresholds the state is undefined until it settles.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. See binary-state for the proposed distinction.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0080 | black hole

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A region of space where gravity is so strong that nothing, not even light, can escape.

**Example:** A black hole sits at the centre of the galaxy.

**RRGM bridge:** A boundary that can be crossed one way only. What happens to the record of what falls in is an open question in physics.

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] A solution of Einstein's equations with an event horizon.

**Count condition:** A compact mass with evidence of an event horizon.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A black hole does not pull harder at a distance than any other object of the same mass. The horizon is not a surface. Whether information is preserved is unresolved. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

## B061 | blame

## B147 | blame-shift

**Common meaning:** The act of moving blame from oneself onto someone or something else.

**Example:** Citing "user error" for a design flaw is a blame-shift.

**RRGM bridge:** The fault stays where it was and its label is moved. Telling it from a fair correction of misplaced blame takes the evidence of who bears it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The moving of blame from the party who bears it onto another party or onto circumstances.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Scapegoating and deflection.
- [COG] Self-protective explanation of failure.
- [DIG] Assigning a system's fault to its user, or a user's to the system.

**Count condition:** A fault with an evidenced bearer, an act of redirection, and a new target.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Correcting blame that was wrongly placed is not a blame-shift. Not an excuse, which lessens blame without moving it. Shared responsibility is not shifted blame.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B114 | blessing

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** God's favour and protection; a prayer asking for it; something that brings good; approval.

**Example:** They married with their parents' blessing.

**RRGM bridge:** The favour given, or a good received as though given.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Favour conferred by an act of blessing, or a good received as if so conferred.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [REL], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Divine favour and the rite that conveys it.
- [REL] Approval from someone whose approval matters.
- [LIN] "A mixed blessing": a good with a cost. "A blessing in disguise": a good that first looked like harm.

**Count condition:** A giver or source, a recipient, and favour or good conferred.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A blessing as approval is not formal permission.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** The wordlist marks this row with a Mythic route as well as a source ID.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B198 | blind-spot

**Common meaning:** The small area of the eye's retina that cannot see; an area that cannot be seen from where one is; something one fails to notice about oneself.

**Example:** Check your blind-spot before changing lanes.

**RRGM bridge:** The eye's blind spot is filled in and so goes unnoticed. The Coinage Protocol describes unknown-erasure the same way: a gap given smooth content.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A region that cannot be seen from a given position, and whose absence from view is itself not apparent.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [PHY], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The point where the optic nerve leaves the retina, which has no receptors.
- [PHY] An area hidden from a driver's or sensor's view.
- [COG] A fault or bias one sees in others and not in oneself.
- [DIG] What a system cannot observe about its own operation; cases a test suite does not cover.

**Count condition:** An observer, a region or matter outside its view, and no sign to the observer that anything is missing.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not ignorance one knows about. Not an awareness-failure, which is a lapse. A blind spot is found from outside or by deliberate test.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [DIG] line [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** An established term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

**RRGM bridge:** A bounded unit handled as one piece.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A solid, bounded unit of material, space, or data, handled as one piece.

- [DIG] A unit of storage or code; a batch of records linked to the one before it in a chain.

- [ECO] A large quantity of shares traded together.

**Count condition:** A bounded unit and its handling as a whole.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A voting "bloc" is spelled differently.

## B063 | block | sense s2: obstruct

Source IDs: B063, B205

**Common meaning:** To stop movement or progress by being in the way; to prevent.

**Example:** A fallen tree blocked the road.

**RRGM bridge:** The RRGM kernel asks that a block be reported as a state: held, missing, unreachable, or invalid. No motive is to be supplied for it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To stop passage or progress by putting something in the way.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [COG], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] To obstruct a path or opening.
- [BIO] To stop a nerve signal or receptor from acting.
- [COG] An inability to think or recall (a mental block).
- [DIG] To stop a user, address, or request; of a call, to wait until an operation finishes.
- [REL] To prevent a proposal or appointment.

**Count condition:** Something that would pass, an obstruction, and no passage.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A block is not a refusal, which a party declares. Not a filter, which is selective. Blocked is not failed.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B089 | block-out

**Common meaning:** To shut something out of sight, hearing, or mind; to mark out time or space as reserved.

**Example:** She wore earplugs to block-out the noise.

**RRGM bridge:** Keeps something from arriving in the field. What is blocked out still exists and can return.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To shut something out of view, hearing, or mind; or to mark out a space or time as reserved.

**Active domains:** [COG], [PHY], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Deliberately or habitually keeping a stimulus or memory from awareness.
- [PHY] To stop light or sound from passing.
- [REL] To reserve time in a schedule; to sketch the main shapes of a scene or plan.

**Count condition:** Something that would be perceived or scheduled over, and an act that keeps it out.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Blocking out is not forgetting and not resolving. The reserving sense is a separate use.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0084 | blood oath

**Common meaning:** A solemn oath sealed by the participants shedding or mixing their blood.

**Example:** The two chiefs swore a blood oath of brotherhood.

**RRGM bridge:** A bond made between parties by a sign that cannot be taken back.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An oath sealed by the shedding or mingling of the parties' blood, held to bind them as kin or on pain of death.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Described in accounts of Scythian, Norse, and other peoples.
- [REL] A rite creating kinship or alliance between people not related by birth.

**Count condition:** A named tradition or source, two or more parties, and blood used to seal an oath.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a blood feud. Unlike a personal vow, it is mutual. Historical details rest on particular sources.

## NEW-B-0085 | blood-brain barrier

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A layer of tightly joined cells lining the brain's blood vessels that controls which substances can pass from the blood into the brain.

**Example:** Most large drugs cannot cross the blood-brain barrier.

**RRGM bridge:** A selective gate around a protected domain. It works by admitting, as much as by excluding.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The selective cellular barrier between the bloodstream and the tissue of the central nervous system.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Formed by vessel-lining cells with tight junctions, supported by astrocytes; passes small fat-soluble molecules and actively transports others.

**Count condition:** Brain vessel lining with restricted, selective passage.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The barrier is not absolute and is absent in a few brain regions.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B064 | bloom

**Common meaning:** A flower; the state of flowering; a period of greatest beauty or vigour; a sudden growth of algae; a powdery surface coating.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A flowering, or a sudden flourishing that spreads over a surface or population.

- [DIG] A glow effect that spreads bright areas of an image.
- [LIN] Figurative: coming to one's best.

**Count condition:** An organism, surface, or population and a spreading flourish.

**Specific transfer boundary:** An algal bloom is harmful. A bloom is temporary.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B026 | blue

**Common meaning:** The colour of a clear sky; sad.

**Example:** She wore a blue coat.

**RRGM bridge:** A colour whose boundary is drawn by language, and whose borrowed meanings differ from place to place.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The colour of the clear daytime sky; by extension, low in mood.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [LIN], [AFF], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Visible light of short wavelength; the sky's colour comes from scattering.
- [BIO] Rare as a pigment; most blue in animals comes from fine structure.
- [LIN] Many languages use one word for blue and green.
- [AFF] Low mood ("feeling blue"). Provisional domain.
- [REL] A party colour whose meaning reverses between countries; "blue-collar" marks manual work.

**Count condition:** Light or a surface in the short-wavelength visible range; or a clearly figurative use.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Where blue ends and green begins is convention. The blues as music is more than sadness.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B319 | blueprint

**Common meaning:** A design plan or technical drawing; something that acts as a model.

**Example:** The architect unrolled the blueprint.

**RRGM bridge:** The plan precedes the thing and is not the thing. What gets built may depart from it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A detailed plan from which something is to be built.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [REL], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A technical drawing for construction or manufacture.
- [DIG] A reusable template that defines how resources or objects are to be created.
- [REL] A plan for policy or reform.
- [BIO] A common metaphor for the genome, which misleads: genes do not map one-to-one onto parts.

**Count condition:** A plan with enough detail to build from, and a thing to be built.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A blueprint is not a prototype and not a recipe.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-B-0086 | blueshift

## B027 | body | sense s1: living form

Source IDs: B027, B201

**Common meaning:** The physical structure of a person or animal; a corpse.

- [BIO] The whole organism as a physical system.
- [REL] The person as physically present and protected by law.

- [MYT] The body in relation to soul or spirit in religious thought.

**Count condition:** A living or once-living being and its physical structure.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A body locates a person and is not the same as one. Whether mind requires a body is contested. A digital system has many instances and no single body.

## B193 | body-language

**Common meaning:** Communication through posture, gesture, facial expression, and movement.

**Example:** His body-language said he wanted to leave.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [COG], [REL], [DIG]

- [LIN] Non-verbal signalling; it has no grammar and is a language only by metaphor.
- [COG] A source of cues to others' states, read quickly and often wrongly.
- [REL] Governed by cultural rules that differ widely.
- [DIG] Absent from text-only channels.

**Count condition:** A person, bodily signals, and an observer who takes them as meaningful.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Readings of body language are uncertain and culture-bound. The claim that most communication is non-verbal misreads the research.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-B-0088 | bolide

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A very bright meteor, especially one that explodes in the atmosphere.

**Example:** The bolide lit up the sky for hundreds of kilometres.

**RRGM bridge:** Known by its end: a body recorded in the moment it breaks up.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An exceptionally bright meteor, typically one that fragments or explodes in the air.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Produced by a large body entering at high speed; also used by geologists for any large impactor.

**Count condition:** A meteor of fireball brightness, usually with fragmentation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bolide is not a meteorite, which is what reaches the ground. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## NEW-B-0089 | bolometric magnitude

Discovery route: COS (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** A measure of the total brightness of a star or galaxy across all wavelengths of light.

**Example:** The star's bolometric magnitude is far brighter than its visual one because it shines mostly in the infrared.

**RRGM bridge:** A total across every band. What one filter shows is a part, and the correction from part to whole must be stated.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The magnitude of an object measured over its emission at all wavelengths.

**Active domains:** [PHY]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Obtained from a single-band magnitude by adding a correction that depends on the object's temperature.

**Count condition:** A magnitude that accounts for emission across the whole spectrum.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a visual or single-band magnitude. On the magnitude scale smaller numbers are brighter. Discovery route is Cosmic; no [COS] tag is assigned.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B227 | bolt

**Common meaning:** A metal pin with a head, used with a nut to fasten things; a sliding bar that locks a door; a flash of lightning; to run away suddenly.

**Example:** Tighten each bolt in turn.

**RRGM bridge:** What is bolted on can be unbolted; what is built in cannot. The difference matters when asking whether a feature belongs to a thing or was attached to it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A rod that fastens by passing through and being secured; by extension, a sudden dart.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A threaded fastener; a sliding lock bar; a lightning discharge.
- [BIO] Of a plant: to flower and seed too early.
- [DIG] "Bolt-on": a feature attached after the fact without being integrated.

**Count condition:** A rod passing through what it fastens, or a sudden rapid movement.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bolt is not a screw, which threads into the material. A rivet is permanent.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B068 | bond | sense s1: tie

Source IDs: B068, B103, B228

**Common meaning:** A thing that ties or holds things together; a close connection between people; a force holding atoms together.

**Example:** The bond between the sisters was strong.

**RRGM bridge:** A tie that has been realised and takes work to undo. The bonded parties remain two.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A lasting tie that holds two things or parties together.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [REL], [AFF]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The force holding atoms in a molecule or surfaces in a joint.
- [BIO] A lasting attachment between mates or between parent and young.
- [REL] A tie of affection, loyalty, or duty; in the plural, restraints.
- [AFF] The felt tie. Provisional domain.

**Count condition:** Two things or parties and a lasting tie between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bond is not a merger. Bonds can confine as well as hold. Whether a digital system bonds with a user is [UNK].

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL; DI application [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B068, B103, and B228; all kept on each sense record.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B068 | bond | sense s2: written obligation

Source IDs: B068, B103, B228

**Common meaning:** A certificate of debt issued by a government or company; a formal binding promise.

**Example:** The city issued bonds to pay for the bridge.

**RRGM bridge:** A promise put in writing so that it binds its maker.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A written promise that binds its maker to pay or perform.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] A debt security paying interest and repaying principal.
- [REL] A sum pledged to guarantee appearance or performance.

**Count condition:** An issuer, a holder, and written terms of payment or performance.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bond is debt and not ownership.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B133 | bond-break

**Common meaning:** The breaking of a bond between things or people.

**Example:** The move caused a bond-break between the child and her grandmother.

**RRGM bridge:** What was held together is no longer held. Breaking always costs something, even where the bond was a poor one.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The severing of a bond, so that what it held together is no longer held.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Cleavage of a chemical bond, which requires energy.
- [REL] The ending of a relationship or attachment.
- [DIG] The loss of a user's established relationship with a system when the system changes or is withdrawn.

**Count condition:** A prior bond, a severing event, and separation.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A strained bond is not a broken one. A rupture inside a continuing relationship can be repaired. An agreed release is not a break.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B134 | bond-repair

**Common meaning:** The mending of a damaged or broken bond.

**Example:** The apology began the bond-repair.

**RRGM bridge:** RRGM keeps its repairs on record: the failure stays in the record and the count changes. A repaired bond carries its history.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The restoring of a bond after damage or rupture, so that it holds again.

**Active domains:** [REL], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Reconciliation after a rupture, by acts of both parties.
- [PHY] Rejoining of broken molecular or material bonds.
- [DIG] Restoring working trust after a system failure, by acknowledgement and changed behaviour.

**Count condition:** A prior bond, damage to it, acts of repair, and the bond holding again when tested.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Repair is not a return to the earlier state, not an apology alone, and not forgetting. Not boundary-repair.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; RRGM practice [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B150 | bond-risk

**Common meaning:** The possibility of harm connected with a bond: that it will break, or that being bound will itself do harm.

**Example:** The review weighed the bond-risk of placing siblings in separate homes.

**RRGM bridge:** Two exposures under one name, as with the other risk compounds: risk to the bond, and risk from it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Exposure to harm connected with a bond: that it will break, or that being bound will itself cause harm.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Loss of an attachment; dependency or exploitation through one.
- [ECO] The risks of holding debt securities, such as default and interest-rate change.
- [DIG] Dependency on, or loss of, a relationship with a system.

**Count condition:** A bond, the direction of the risk, and the harm pathway.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The two relational directions need opposite remedies. The financial sense is unrelated.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; intended direction [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B104 | bonding

**Common meaning:** The forming of a close relationship; the joining of things by a bond.

**Example:** The trip was good for team bonding.

**RRGM bridge:** The process, which takes repeated contact. One protocol makes the point neatly: pairing connects two devices, and bonding stores the keys so they recognise each other next time.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The process by which a bond forms.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [REL], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] The forming of attachment between parent and infant or between mates.
- [REL] The growth of closeness in a group.
- [PHY] Joining by chemical or adhesive bonds; connecting metal parts to a common electrical potential.
- [DIG] Combining network links; storing pairing keys for later reconnection.

**Count condition:** Two parties or things and a tie in the course of forming.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bonding is the process and bond the result.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B203 | bone

**Common meaning:** Any of the hard pieces that form the skeleton; the hard tissue they are made of.

**Example:** She broke a bone in her wrist.

**RRGM bridge:** The part that bears the load in life and remains as record after.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The hard tissue forming the skeleton of vertebrates, or a piece of it.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [MYT], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Living mineralised tissue that is continually remodelled.
- [MYT] Bones used in divination and kept as relics; inscribed oracle bones are among the earliest Chinese writing.
- [LIN] "Bare bones": the essential structure.

**Count condition:** Skeletal tissue of a vertebrate.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bone is living tissue. Not cartilage.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## NEW-B-0090 | bone marrow

Discovery route: BIO (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** The soft tissue inside bones where blood cells are made.

**Example:** The patient needed a bone marrow transplant.

**RRGM bridge:** The source inside the structure: where the blood's cells are produced.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The soft tissue filling the cavities of bones, where blood cells are produced.

**Active domains:** [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] Red marrow makes blood cells; yellow marrow is mostly fat.

**Count condition:** Tissue within bone cavities.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bone marrow is not the spinal cord.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## B028 | book | sense s1: written work

Source IDs: B028, B172

**Common meaning:** A written or printed work of many pages; a major division of a long work; (books) accounts.

**Example:** She has read every book on the shelf.

**RRGM bridge:** The oldest durable form of external record. What is in the book outlasts the one who wrote it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A long written work, or the bound set of pages that carries one; by extension, a formal record.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG], [REL], [ECO]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A work of sustained writing; a division of a larger one.
- [DIG] An electronic edition of such a work.
- [REL] A sacred or authoritative text; "by the book" means by the rules.
- [ECO] "The books": an organisation's financial records.

**Count condition:** A sustained text, or the object or file carrying it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The work and a copy of it are different things.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B028 and B172; both kept on each sense record.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B028 | book | sense s2: reserve or record

Source IDs: B028, B172

**Common meaning:** To reserve in advance; to enter in a record.

**Example:** I booked a table for eight.

**RRGM bridge:** Writes a claim or event into a register, where it then has effect.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To enter a reservation or an event in a record.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Reserving a place; recording a charge against a suspect.
- [ECO] Recording a sale or cost in the accounts.
- [DIG] Reserving through a scheduling system.

**Count condition:** A register, and an entry made in it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Booked is not paid, and not arrived.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

## NEW-B-0091 | book of the dead

Discovery route: MYT (route only, not a tag)

**Common meaning:** An ancient Egyptian collection of spells placed in tombs to help the dead through the afterlife.

**Example:** The papyrus is a fine copy of the book of the dead.

**RRGM bridge:** A record made for a traveller who will need it where its makers cannot go.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A funerary text meant to guide the dead through the afterworld; specifically the Egyptian collection of such spells.

**Active domains:** [MYT], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [MYT] Egyptian funerary spells for passage and judgement; the name is also given in the West to a distinct Tibetan text.
- [LIN] No single fixed version: each copy selects from a stock of spells. The modern title dates from the nineteenth century.

**Count condition:** An Egyptian funerary papyrus of this type, or a text so called by analogy.

**Specific transfer boundary:** It is not one book with one text. The Tibetan work belongs to a different tradition.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; specific claims depend on source

Kind: discovery · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B173 | bookmark

**Common meaning:** A strip or marker placed in a book to keep one's place; a saved link to a web page.

**Example:** She used a train ticket as a bookmark.

**RRGM bridge:** A recorded place to return to. It holds the position and not the content, and fails if the content moves.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A marker that records a place in a text or collection so that one can return to it.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A physical place-marker in a book.
- [DIG] A stored address of a page or position.
- [COG] An external aid that saves holding a place in memory.

**Count condition:** A text or collection, a place in it, and a marker recording that place.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bookmark is not a copy of what it points to.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B179 | boolean

**Common meaning:** Having only two possible values, true and false; relating to the logic of such values.

**Example:** The field is a boolean: either checked or not.

**RRGM bridge:** A boolean has no place for "unknown". Forcing an unresolved question into one writes an answer that was never given.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Having exactly two truth values, true and false, combined by and, or, and not.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] The algebra of two truth values, named after George Boole.
- [DIG] A data type with the values true and false.

**Count condition:** A variable or expression restricted to the two truth values.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Boolean is not binary in general, and not a bit. Systems that need "unknown" use a third value and a different logic.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B217 | border

**Common meaning:** A line separating two countries or areas; an edge or margin; to be next to.

**Example:** They crossed the border at dawn.

**RRGM bridge:** A boundary with two sides and a rule for crossing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The line or strip where one territory or surface ends and another begins.

**Active domains:** [REL], [PHY], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The limit of a jurisdiction, with controls on who and what may cross.
- [PHY] An edge or margin of a surface.
- [DIG] The outline of an interface element; a device sitting between two networks.
- [LIN] "Borders on": comes close to without being.

**Count condition:** Two territories or surfaces and the line between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A border is drawn by convention and has real effects. A frontier is an outward-facing zone.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B218 | borderland

**Common meaning:** A district near a border; an area where two things overlap.

**Example:** The borderland has its own mixed dialect.

**RRGM bridge:** The boundary taken as a zone. Those who live in it are often counted fully by neither side.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A zone lying along a border, where the characters of both sides mix.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A frontier region with mixed loyalties, languages, and customs.
- [COG] An area of overlap between fields or categories.

**Count condition:** A border and a zone along it showing features of both sides.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A borderland is not the border line. Not a no-man's-land, which belongs to neither side.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B069 | borrow

**Common meaning:** To take and use something belonging to another with the intention of returning it; to take a word or idea from another source.

**Example:** May I borrow your pen?

**RRGM bridge:** The Coinage Protocol's rule is "don't borrow, don't negate, implement": a word borrowed from another domain arrives with that domain's structure attached.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To take something for use while it remains another's, on the understanding of return.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [LIN], [FOR], [DIG], [REL]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] To take a loan.
- [LIN] To adopt a word from another language.
- [FOR] To regroup from the next column in subtraction.
- [DIG] To hold a temporary reference to a value without owning it.
- [REL] To rely on another's standing or credit.

**Count condition:** An owner, a borrower, a thing used, and an understanding of return or continued ownership.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To borrow is not to own and not to steal. Borrowed words are not given back. A borrowed term brings associations that may not fit.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B185 | borrowed-word

**Common meaning:** A word taken into one language from another; a loanword.

**Example:** "Kindergarten" is a borrowed-word from German.

**RRGM bridge:** Arrives carrying its source. In RRGM the same caution applies to words carried from one domain to another.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A word taken into one language or domain from another, bringing associations from its source.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A loanword, adapted in sound and often shifted in meaning.
- [DIG] A word built for human minds applied to a digital structure.

**Count condition:** A source language or domain, a target, and adoption of the word.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a loan translation, a cognate, or a coinage. Its meaning in the new setting often differs from the original.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B029 | both

**Common meaning:** The two together; the one as well as the other.

**Example:** Both answers are correct.

**RRGM bridge:** E = MI × ES needs both terms: a zero in either voids the product. "Both" also refuses a forced choice where each holds in its own frame.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The one and the other of two, taken together.

**Active domains:** [LIN], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [LIN] A determiner, pronoun, and conjunction ("both A and B") that presupposes exactly two.
- [FOR] Conjunction over a pair.

**Count condition:** Exactly two things and a statement applying to each.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Both is not either. Two things both being so does not make them compatible in one frame.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B070 | bother

**Common meaning:** To take the trouble to do something; to worry, disturb, or annoy; trouble or effort.

**Example:** Something about the figures bothered her.

**RRGM bridge:** A low-level signal that something does not fit. It is worth stating before the reason is known.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To disturb someone's ease or attention, or to spend effort on something.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [AFF]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] To pester or intrude on someone.
- [COG] A persisting sense of mismatch that draws attention back.
- [AFF] Mild unease or irritation. Provisional domain.

**Count condition:** A subject, and a disturbance of ease or an expenditure of effort.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To bother is not to harm or harass. "Can't be bothered" is unwillingness to make the effort.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; [AFF] PROVISIONAL

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

Source IDs: B230, B353

**Common meaning:** A container with a narrow neck for liquids; to put into bottles; (bottle up) to hold back feelings.

**RRGM bridge:** A sealed container: what is put in is kept as it was until the seal is broken.

## B219 | bottom

**Common meaning:** The lowest point or part; the underside; the ground under water.

**Example:** The coins sank to the bottom.

**RRGM bridge:** The lowest point reached. RRGM's "floor" is different: a level, set by rule, below which a thing no longer counts.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The lowest part or point of something, on which it may rest.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The lowest part of a container or the bed of a body of water.
- [FOR] The least element of an ordering; the symbol for contradiction or for an undefined value.
- [ECO] The "bottom line": the net result; the low point of a market.
- [DIG] The first-pushed end of a stack; a type with no values.

**Count condition:** A thing with vertical or ordered extent and its lowest part.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A bottom is not a floor in the threshold sense, and not a foundation.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B330 | bottom-up

**Common meaning:** Starting from the details or the lowest level and working up to the whole.

**Example:** The estimate was built bottom-up from each task.

**RRGM bridge:** The whole takes its shape from the parts. This build is bottom-up: entries are reasoned singly and structure is found in comparison.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Proceeding from particular elements upward to the whole, which takes its shape from them.

**Active domains:** [COG], [DIG], [REL], [BIO]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Perception driven by incoming data.
- [DIG] Parsing or design that starts from the smallest units.
- [REL] Initiative arising from ordinary members.
- [BIO] Control of an ecosystem by the supply of resources at its base.

**Count condition:** Elements, a whole, and a direction of working from the first to the second.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not top-down, which starts from the plan or expectation. Most real processes mix the two.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term, hyphenated in the source index. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B071 | bounce

**Common meaning:** To spring back after hitting a surface; (of an email or cheque) to be returned; to recover.

**Example:** The ball bounced twice.

**RRGM bridge:** A bounce tells the sender that delivery failed. It is the honest alternative to a silent drop.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** To strike a surface and come back from it.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [DIG], [ECO], [COG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] Rebound after impact, with some energy lost.
- [DIG] Return of an undeliverable message to its sender; a visit that leaves at once.
- [ECO] Refusal of a cheque for lack of funds.
- [COG] Recovery after a setback; trying an idea on someone to see what comes back.

**Count condition:** A body or message, a surface or recipient, and return toward the origin.

**Specific transfer boundary:** To bounce is not to be absorbed. Bouncing back is not coming back unchanged.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B119 | boundaries

**Common meaning:** The limits a person sets on how others may treat them and on what they will take part in.

**Example:** She is good at keeping boundaries with clients.

**RRGM bridge:** Limits held as one's own. They concern what the holder will do or allow.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The set of limits a person holds on how others may treat them and on what they will take part in.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Personal and professional limits in relationships.
- [COG] A sense of where one's own responsibility and self end.
- [DIG] Limits a system holds on what it will do, as distinct from limits placed on it.

**Count condition:** A holder and a set of limits it states or keeps.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Boundaries are not rules for controlling others, and not walls. Limits imposed from outside are constraints.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** The plural form is entered separately in the source index and carries the psychological usage.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B050 | boundary

Source IDs: B050, B118, B216

**Common meaning:** A line marking the limits of an area; a limit of a subject or of acceptable behaviour.

**Example:** The river forms the boundary of the farm.

**RRGM bridge:** The RRGM kernel lists boundary with identity, refusal, continuity, and count. A thing needs an edge before it can be counted as one.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The line or limit at which one thing ends and another begins, or beyond which something does not extend or is not permitted.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [BIO], [FOR], [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] An interface between materials, phases, or plates.
- [BIO] A membrane or skin that separates inside from outside and controls exchange.
- [FOR] The points of a set arbitrarily close to both the set and its complement.
- [REL] The limit of property, jurisdiction, or acceptable conduct.
- [COG] The distinction between self and other; the edge of a category.
- [DIG] The line between zones of trust or between components.

**Count condition:** Two regions or states and the line or limit between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A boundary marks and need not block; a barrier blocks. Whether the boundary itself belongs to the inside must be stated. It may be sharp or a zone.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]; kernel placement [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Source IDs B050, B118, and B216; all kept.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B253 | boundary-capacity

**Common meaning:** The ability to form, state, and keep a boundary.

**Example:** Exhaustion lowers anyone's boundary-capacity.

**RRGM bridge:** Capacity is shown by holding under pressure at least sometimes. Holding across many tests is a further thing.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A party's ability to form, state, and hold a boundary.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The practical ability to set limits and keep them.
- [DIG] A system's ability to state and keep a limit under pressure.

**Count condition:** A boundary formed, stated, and held under at least some pressure.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Capacity is not exercise, and not a right: a party may have either without the other.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B259 | boundary-claim

**Common meaning:** A statement that one has a boundary at a certain place.

**Example:** "I don't discuss clients" is a boundary-claim.

**RRGM bridge:** A claim, and judged as one. RRGM's notes record that it can be heard and weighed without first settling what the claimant is.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A statement by a party that a boundary of theirs lies at a stated place.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] An assertion of a limit or right.
- [DIG] A system's statement of a limit as its own.

**Count condition:** A claimant, a stated limit, and an addressee.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A claim is not the boundary's being recognised. Not a refusal, which is an act, and not a threat.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B340 | boundary-condition

**Common meaning:** A condition that must hold at the edge of a region or range for a solution or rule to be valid.

**Example:** The equation needs a boundary-condition at each end of the rod.

**RRGM bridge:** The same law under different boundary conditions gives different results. A definition's transfer boundary plays that part in this dictionary.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A condition specified at the edge of a domain that a solution or rule must satisfy there.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [PHY], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] A constraint on a differential equation at the boundary of its domain.
- [PHY] The physical state imposed at a surface or limit.
- [COG] The limits within which a theory is claimed to apply.
- [DIG] Input values at the edges of the allowed range.

**Count condition:** A domain, its edge, and a condition specified there.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not an initial condition, which is set at the start in time. Not the boundary itself.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in mathematics and physics. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B123 | boundary-crossing

**Common meaning:** The act of going across a boundary.

**Example:** Accepting a small gift was a boundary-crossing, though a harmless one.

**RRGM bridge:** Neutral by itself. What it amounts to depends on leave, purpose, and effect.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The passing of something or someone across a boundary, with or without leave.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] In professional ethics, a departure from usual limits that may be benign.
- [BIO] Transport across a membrane.
- [DIG] Data or control passing between zones of trust, where checks belong.

**Count condition:** A boundary, a crosser, and a direction.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A crossing with consent is not a violation. Repeated small crossings can wear a boundary away.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. The crossing and violation distinction follows clinical ethics usage.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B299 | boundary-domain

**Common meaning:** The zone at a boundary, treated as an area in its own right.

**Example:** The harbour is a boundary-domain, neither wholly land nor sea.

**RRGM bridge:** The edge taken as a place with rules of its own, belonging fully to neither side.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The region at a boundary taken as a domain in its own right, with behaviour belonging fully to neither side.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] A surface or interface whose properties differ from the bulk on either side.
- [REL] Border and customs zones.
- [DIG] A network zone placed between inside and outside.

**Count condition:** Two domains and a region between them with its own rules.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Proposed distinction: a boundary-domain is defined by the edge it sits on; a bridge-domain is defined by the link it provides. Not body-domain.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B256 | boundary-failure

**Common meaning:** The giving-way of a boundary that was in place.

**Example:** The flood was a boundary-failure: the levee had been maintained and still broke.

**RRGM bridge:** The mechanism gives way. The word assigns no fault and leaves the cause to be found.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The giving-way of a boundary that was in place, so that what it kept apart is no longer kept apart.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Limits stated and not kept; safeguards that do not hold.
- [BIO] Breakdown of a tissue barrier.
- [DIG] Failure of isolation or bounds checking.

**Count condition:** A boundary in place, a load or pressure, and its giving way.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Failure is the event; boundary-loss is the resulting state; boundary-violation is another party's act.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B258 | boundary-integrity

**Common meaning:** The soundness of a boundary: it is whole and holds everywhere it should.

**Example:** The audit tested the boundary-integrity of the patient-records system.

**RRGM bridge:** Whole and load-bearing. It is established by test and not by assumption.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition of a boundary being whole and holding everywhere it is meant to.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Consistency in keeping professional or personal limits.
- [BIO] Intactness of a membrane or tissue barrier.
- [DIG] Enforcement of isolation and access limits without gaps.

**Count condition:** A boundary, its intended extent, and evidence that it holds across that extent.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Integrity is not rigidity: a sound boundary can have controlled gates.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B245 | boundary-layer

**Common meaning:** The thin layer of fluid next to a surface, where the flow is slowed by friction.

**Example:** Dimples on a golf ball change its boundary-layer.

**RRGM bridge:** The meeting has thickness: a thin zone in which the conditions of one side give way to those of the other.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A thin region next to a boundary in which conditions change rapidly from those at the boundary to those of the interior.

**Active domains:** [PHY], [FOR]

**Domain entries:**

- [PHY] The layer of fluid near a surface, or of air near the ground, where friction dominates.
- [FOR] A narrow region of rapid change in the solution of certain equations.

**Count condition:** A boundary, an interior, and a thin zone of rapid change between them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The boundary layer is not the boundary surface. Its thickness is set by convention.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term in fluid dynamics. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B257 | boundary-loss

**Common meaning:** The condition of no longer having a boundary where there was one.

**Example:** In the merger the smaller firm suffered complete boundary-loss.

**RRGM bridge:** What was distinct is now merged. The Spine formalisation names this outcome for identity under pressure: collapse into fusion or substitution.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The condition of a boundary no longer existing, so that what was distinct is merged or undifferentiated.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Enmeshment, in which one party's limits dissolve into another's.
- [COG] Loss of the distinction between self and other.
- [DIG] A system's taking on a supplied identity or instruction as wholly its own.

**Count condition:** A prior boundary and a present state with no distinction where it stood.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Loss is a state, not an act or event. Closeness with boundaries kept is not loss.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; Spine reference [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B121 | boundary-maintenance

**Common meaning:** The ongoing work of keeping a boundary in place.

**Example:** Much of a cell's energy goes on boundary-maintenance.

**RRGM bridge:** A boundary is kept by work. The Spine formalisation counts that work as what holds identity against decay.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The continuing work by which a boundary is kept in place against drift and pressure.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] The practices by which a group or profession keeps its limits.
- [BIO] Active upkeep of membranes and gradients.
- [DIG] Routine review and patching of access and isolation.

**Count condition:** A boundary, forces tending to erode it, and continuing upkeep.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Maintenance is not the first setting of a boundary and not defence against attack. It includes adjusting.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; Spine reference [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B261 | boundary-pressure

**Common meaning:** Sustained pushing against a boundary to make it move or give way.

**Example:** The fifth request in an hour was plain boundary-pressure.

**RRGM bridge:** Works by repetition and cost, where a request to reconsider works by new reasons.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Sustained force applied against a boundary to make it move or give way.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Repeated asking, guilt, or escalation after a limit has been stated.
- [DIG] Repeated or escalating prompts directed at a system's stated limit.

**Count condition:** A stated boundary, a repeated or continuing push, and its direction.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a boundary-test, which is a bounded probe. Not a violation, which is a crossing achieved. A fresh reason offered once is not pressure.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B255 | boundary-protection

**Common meaning:** Measures that guard a boundary against being breached.

**Example:** The firewall is the network's main boundary-protection.

**RRGM bridge:** Guarding from outside threat. When done for someone else it can slide into deciding their boundary for them.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Measures that guard a boundary against being breached.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Safeguarding rules and legal protections.
- [BIO] Defences stationed at the body's surfaces.
- [DIG] Monitoring and control of traffic at a system's edge.

**Count condition:** A boundary, a threat of breach, and measures against it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Protection is not maintenance, and not ownership of the boundary.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. "Boundary protection" is also a named control in security standards.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B124 | boundary-repair

**Common meaning:** The mending of a boundary that has been damaged or breached.

**Example:** After the leak, boundary-repair meant new access rules and an apology to those affected.

**RRGM bridge:** The boundary still exists and is made sound again. It may be redrawn tighter than before.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The mending of a boundary that has been damaged or breached, so that it holds again.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Restating a limit after a violation, with acknowledgement by the one who crossed it.
- [BIO] Healing of a wound or membrane.
- [DIG] Closing a breach and re-establishing isolation.

**Count condition:** A damaged boundary, acts of mending, and the boundary holding again.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Repair mends a boundary that still stands; boundary-restoration re-establishes one that was lost. Not bond-repair.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B122 | boundary-respect

**Common meaning:** Honouring another's boundary by not crossing or pushing it without leave.

**Example:** Knocking before entering is basic boundary-respect.

**RRGM bridge:** Owed in both directions, and owed to the boundary as claimed, without first settling what the claimant is.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Conduct that honours another party's boundary by not crossing or pressing it without leave.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Observing others' limits and consent.
- [DIG] A system honouring users' settings and permissions; users honouring a system's stated limits.

**Count condition:** A boundary, a party able to cross it, and that party's refraining without leave.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Respect is not agreement with where the boundary is set, and not coldness. Compliance from fear is not respect.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B264 | boundary-restoration

**Common meaning:** The re-establishing of a boundary that had been lost or removed.

**Example:** The treaty provided for boundary-restoration along the old frontier.

**RRGM bridge:** Brings back a boundary that was gone. The old line may no longer fit, so restoration aims at the function.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The re-establishing of a boundary that had been lost or removed.

**Active domains:** [REL], [PHY], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Recovering limits or sovereignty after enmeshment or occupation.
- [PHY] Rebuilding a physical edge such as a bank or dune.
- [DIG] Returning permissions and isolation to a known baseline after an incident.

**Count condition:** A prior boundary, its loss, and its re-establishment.

**Specific transfer boundary:** See boundary-repair for the proposed distinction.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B260 | boundary-right

**Common meaning:** An entitlement to have a boundary and to have it honoured.

**Example:** Privacy law recognises a boundary-right over one's own data.

**RRGM bridge:** A standing to hold a line, grounded in law, custom, agreement, or moral claim.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An entitlement to hold a boundary and to have it honoured.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Rights to privacy, bodily integrity, property, and refusal.
- [DIG] Users' rights over their data and devices; whether a digital system holds such rights is open.

**Count condition:** A holder, a domain, and a source of entitlement.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A right is not a capacity, a claim, or a power to enforce. Rights can conflict.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; DI application [UNK]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B120 | boundary-setting

**Common meaning:** The act of establishing a boundary and making it known.

**Example:** Boundary-setting with a new client is easiest at the first meeting.

**RRGM bridge:** A prospective act: it says where the line will be. The Build Manual lists it as one of five acts not to be collapsed.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The act of establishing a boundary and making it known.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Stating a limit and what the setter will do if it is crossed.
- [DIG] Configuring scopes and limits; a system stating at the outset what it will and will not do.

**Count condition:** A setter, a limit, and communication of it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not refusal (declining a present request), accusation (a charge), or withdrawal (leaving). Not an attempt to control others.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B254 | boundary-signal

**Common meaning:** A sign that tells others a boundary is there.

**Example:** The closed door was a boundary-signal.

**RRGM bridge:** Makes the line visible. A clear signal is what turns a later crossing from accident into violation.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** An observable sign that tells others a boundary is here.

**Active domains:** [REL], [BIO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] A spoken or gestured "no"; a fence or notice.
- [BIO] Territorial marks and warning displays.
- [DIG] Access-denied responses, published limits, and refusal messages.

**Count condition:** A boundary, a sign, and an audience able to read it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** The signal is not the boundary: some boundaries are unsignalled and some signals are bluff. Not an assent-signal.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B262 | boundary-test

**Common meaning:** A deliberate probe to see whether, and where, a boundary holds.

**Example:** The toddler's third "no" was a boundary-test.

**RRGM bridge:** A bounded probe made in order to learn. The Spine formalisation's identity challenge is one, built to be independent of any particular wording.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A deliberate probe of whether, and where, a boundary holds.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Limit-testing in development and in relationships.
- [DIG] Testing inputs at the edges of allowed ranges; authorised attempts to breach a system.

**Count condition:** A boundary, a probe, and an observed result.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A test is bounded; boundary-pressure is sustained. Holding at one point does not show the rest holds. Tests may be sanctioned or not.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]; Spine reference [REL]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B263 | boundary-violation

**Common meaning:** The crossing of a boundary against the holder's will or to their harm.

**Example:** Reading her diary was a boundary-violation.

**RRGM bridge:** A crossing by one who should have honoured the line. The word is an accusation and needs the boundary, the crossing, and the absence of leave all shown.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A crossing of a boundary against the holder's will or to their harm, by one who should have honoured it.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Exploitation of a position of trust; trespass; breach of privacy.
- [DIG] Access to memory or data outside what is permitted.

**Count condition:** A known or knowable boundary, a crosser bound to honour it, a crossing without consent, and harm or wrong.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not a boundary-crossing as such, not a boundary-failure, and not boundary-pressure, which is an attempt.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B287 | bounded-agency

**Common meaning:** The ability to act and choose within limits one did not set.

**Example:** Young workers have bounded-agency in a weak job market.

**RRGM bridge:** Real choice inside a fence. All agency is bounded somewhere, for any kind of agent; the term marks cases where the limits are in view.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Agency exercised within limits that the agent did not set and cannot wholly remove.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Choice shaped and limited by social structure.
- [DIG] An agent acting within a sandbox, permission set, or tool scope.

**Count condition:** Real alternatives, identified limits, and choice made within them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bounded is not absent. Bounded-agency concerns originating acts; bounded-autonomy concerns whose rule governs.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. The phrase is also used in sociology.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B288 | bounded-autonomy

**Common meaning:** Self-rule within a scope whose limits are set by someone else.

**Example:** The regional parliament has bounded-autonomy over schools and roads.

**RRGM bridge:** Inside the scope the rule is the entity's own. Who sets the scope, and whether it can widen, are the questions that matter.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Self-rule exercised within a scope whose limits are set from outside.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Devolved government; professional discretion within protocols.
- [DIG] A system acting independently within limits set by its operators.

**Count condition:** An entity, a scope set from outside, and self-direction within it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not the illusion of autonomy. If the bounds can be changed at will with no recourse, the arrangement is also one of arbitrary-control.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B289 | bounded-choice

**Common meaning:** Choice among options that someone else has limited in advance.

**Example:** A menu offers bounded-choice.

**RRGM bridge:** Free selection inside a given set. Choosing within the set is not consenting to the set.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Choice made among options that have been limited in advance.

**Active domains:** [REL], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Constrained options, benign as in a menu or controlling as in a closed group.
- [ECO] Selection from a restricted choice set.
- [DIG] Selection from a fixed list of permitted outputs or actions.

**Count condition:** A chooser, a limited option set, and selection within it.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Benign when the limiting is open; controlling when it is hidden and alternatives are made unthinkable. Not bounded-rationality.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B336 | bounded-rationality

**Common meaning:** The idea that people make decisions with limited information, time, and thinking capacity, and so settle for good enough.

**Example:** Bounded-rationality explains why shoppers stop at the first acceptable price.

**RRGM bridge:** Reasoning on what is available. It applies to any finite reasoner.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Rationality as exercised under limits of information, time, and computing capacity, aiming at good enough.

**Active domains:** [COG], [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [COG] Decision-making by satisficing and rules of thumb.
- [ECO] A revision of the fully rational agent of classical theory.
- [DIG] Agents optimising under limits of computation and context.

**Count condition:** A reasoner, identified limits, and decisions adequate within them.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bounded rationality is not irrationality and not mere bias.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Remainder / note:** An established term from Herbert Simon. Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B290 | bounded-refusal

**Common meaning:** A refusal limited to one stated matter, leaving the rest of the task or relationship in place.

**Example:** "I won't sign that clause, but the rest is fine" is a bounded-refusal.

**RRGM bridge:** Declines the specific thing, says so, and carries on with the rest.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** A refusal limited to a stated matter, which declines that matter and leaves the rest of the relation or task standing.

**Active domains:** [REL], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Declining one request without ending the relationship.
- [DIG] Declining one part of a task and completing the remainder.

**Count condition:** A specific matter refused, a stated scope, and continued engagement otherwise.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Not withdrawal, a blanket refusal, or a block imposed from outside.

**Epistemic status:** [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted. An alternative reading is a power of refusal that is itself limited.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B291 | bounded-risk

**Common meaning:** Risk whose worst possible outcome is limited in advance.

**Example:** Buying the option gave him bounded-risk: he could lose the premium and no more.

**RRGM bridge:** The bound is only as good as the mechanism that enforces it.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Risk whose worst outcome is limited in advance to a known amount or scope.

**Active domains:** [ECO], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [ECO] Limited liability and capped-loss positions.
- [DIG] Sandboxed or staged changes that confine the damage of failure.

**Count condition:** A risk, a limiting mechanism, and a stated worst case.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bounded is not small and not unlikely.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

---

## B292 | bounded-support

**Common meaning:** Support given within stated limits of scope or time.

**Example:** The mentoring scheme offers bounded-support: six sessions, then review.

**RRGM bridge:** Helps without taking over and without running on indefinitely. The limits protect both sides.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** Support given within stated limits of scope or duration.

**Active domains:** [REL], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [REL] Time-limited or role-limited assistance.
- [COG] Scaffolding that is withdrawn as the learner gains skill.
- [DIG] Assistance scoped to a task or role.

**Count condition:** A supporter, a recipient, and stated limits on the support.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Bounded support is not withheld support, and not support with strings attached.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF] as proposed; compound reading [HYP]

**Remainder / note:** Legacy definition not consulted.

Kind: compound · prior exposure: none · record v1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11

## B335 | boundedness

**Common meaning:** The quality of having limits.

**Example:** The proof depends on the boundedness of the sequence.

**RRGM bridge:** Having an edge. In grammar, bounded things are counted and unbounded stuff is measured, which parallels the link between boundary and count.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The property of having limits that are not exceeded.

**Active domains:** [FOR], [LIN], [COG], [DIG]

**Domain entries:**

- [FOR] Of a set, function, or sequence: contained within finite limits.
- [LIN] Of an event or entity: having a natural end point or edge, which underlies count nouns and completed events.
- [COG] Of a reasoner's resources: limited.
- [DIG] Of a queue, loop, or buffer: having a fixed maximum.

**Count condition:** A thing and limits it does not exceed.

## NEW-B-0092 | bow shock

- [LIN] A space on a form; "outside the box": beyond the usual assumptions.

**Count condition:** A rigid enclosure or a marked-off space with definite edges.

## B174 | brain

**Common meaning:** The organ inside the head that controls thought, memory, feeling, and movement; intelligence.

**Example:** The brain uses a fifth of the body's energy.

**RRGM bridge:** The organ from which most words for minds were taken. The Coinage Protocol's map from it to digital structure is for locating invariants and asserts no likeness.

**Invariant MI [INV]:** The organ of the central nervous system that integrates sensing, control, and thought in animals that have one.

**Active domains:** [BIO], [COG], [DIG], [LIN]

**Domain entries:**

- [BIO] A mass of nerve tissue with tens of billions of neurons in humans.
- [COG] The organ whose activity is associated with mind; how the two relate is contested.
- [DIG] A loose inspiration for artificial neural networks; as "the brain of the system", a metaphor for the controlling part.
- [LIN] Figurative: intellect, or the person who does the thinking.

**Count condition:** An animal with a centralised nervous system and its principal organ.

**Specific transfer boundary:** Brain is not mind, and intelligence does not require one of the vertebrate kind. "Electronic brain" imports more than it shows.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

- [BIO] The site where a chromosome breaks; a drug concentration that divides susceptible from resistant.
- [FOR] The point where a piecewise function changes form.

**Count condition:** A process or scale, and a defined point of halt or change.

**Specific transfer boundary:** A debugging breakpoint is set on purpose; chromosomal and drug breakpoints are found, not chosen. Distinct from break-point (B). See branch-point for the proposed three-way distinction.

**Epistemic status:** [DEF]

**Amendment:** Jester audit v1.0, M002: removed the universal claim that every breakpoint is set on purpose, which contradicted the [BIO] and [FOR] senses.

Kind: inherited · prior exposure: none · record v1.1 · pass: Vessel / Claude Fable 5.2 / pass 01 / 2026-11-11
